is part of the fixed point argument since the RHS of (
4.4
) depend on
\widecheck{u}
and
\widecheck{X}
themselves. The parameter will thus depend on
\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)
and we denote it
\mathfrak{p}\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)
. To ensure
r^{-q-\delta}
asymptotic
r
-decay, we will choose
\mathfrak{p}
so that

•

the Hamiltonian orthogonality conditions hold

\displaystyle\left\langle A\left(\mathfrak{p}\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),w_{j,\ell}\right\rangle=0,

(4.7)

for
1\leq j\leq q
and
-(j-1)\leq\ell\leq j-1
,

•

the momentum orthogonality conditions hold

\displaystyle\left\langle B\left(\mathfrak{p}\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),W_{1,0,k}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=0,

(4.8)

\displaystyle\left\langle B\left(\mathfrak{p}\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),\Omega^{\pm}_{ab}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=0,

(4.9)

\displaystyle\left\langle B\left(\mathfrak{p}\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),W_{j,\ell,k}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=0,

(4.10)

for
3\leq j\leq q
,
-(j-1)\leq\ell\leq j-1
,
k=1,2,3
and
a,b=1,2,3
.

Remark 4.3
.

If
q=1
, we only need to ensure the orthogonality conditions (
4.7
) for
j=1
and (
4.8
). If
q=2
, we only need to ensure the orthogonality conditions (
4.7
) for
j=1,2
, (
4.8
) and (
4.9
).

We give a brief outline of the proof of Theorem
3.8
.

•

In Section
4.2
, we expand
A\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)
and
B\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)
and identify main terms and error terms.

•

In Section
4.3
, we use these expansions to compute the projections appearing in the orthogonality conditions above.

•

In Section
4.4
, we construct a map
\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)\mapsto\mathfrak{p}\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)\in P
such that the orthogonality conditions hold.

•

In Section
4.5
, we solve the constraint equations with the parameter
\mathfrak{p}\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)
previously constructed.

4.2
Expansions of
A\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)
and
B\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)

In this section, we give two propositions expanding first the main terms in
A\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)
and
B\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)
, i.e.
{\mathcal{H}}(\bar{g}(\mathfrak{p}),\bar{\pi}(\mathfrak{p}))
and
{\mathcal{M}}(\bar{g}(\mathfrak{p}),\bar{\pi}(\mathfrak{p}))
, and then the remainders
{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathcal{H}}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)
and
{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathcal{M}}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)
.

Proposition 4.4
.

If
\mathfrak{p}\in P
is such that

\displaystyle m+\left\|\breve{g}\right\|_{W^{2,\infty}}+\left\|\breve{\pi}\right\|_{W^{1,\infty}}\leq\frac{1}{2},

(4.11)

then we have

\displaystyle{\mathcal{H}}(\bar{g}(\mathfrak{p}),\bar{\pi}(\mathfrak{p}))

\displaystyle={\mathcal{H}}\left(e+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}\right)+D{\mathcal{H}}[e,0]\left(\breve{g}\right)

(4.12)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}D^{2}{\mathcal{H}}[e,0]\left(\left(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi}\right),\left(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi}\right)\right)+\breve{g}^{ij}\Delta\widecheck{g}_{ij}+\mathrm{Err}_{1}(\mathfrak{p}),

\displaystyle{\mathcal{M}}(\bar{g}(\mathfrak{p}),\bar{\pi}(\mathfrak{p}))_{i}

\displaystyle={\mathcal{M}}\left(e+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}\right)_{i}+D{\mathcal{M}}[e,0](\breve{\pi})_{i}

(4.13)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}D^{2}{\mathcal{M}}[e,0]((\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi}),(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi}))_{i}-\breve{g}^{k\ell}\partial_{k}\widecheck{\pi}_{\ell i}+\mathrm{Err}_{2}(\mathfrak{p})_{i},

where the error terms satisfy

\displaystyle|\mathrm{Err}_{i}(\mathfrak{p})|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\gamma}\frac{\varepsilon m}{(1+r)^{q+\delta+3}}

(4.14)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\varepsilon}{(1+r)^{q+\delta}}\left(|\nabla\widecheck{g}|^{2}+|\widecheck{\pi}|^{2}\right)+\left(\left\|\breve{g}\right\|_{W^{2,\infty}}^{2}+\left\|\breve{\pi}\right\|_{W^{1,\infty}}^{2}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{1\leq r\leq 10\}}

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\left\|\breve{g}\right\|_{W^{2,\infty}}+\left\|\breve{\pi}\right\|_{W^{1,\infty}}\right)\left(|\widecheck{g}|+|\nabla\widecheck{g}|+|\widecheck{\pi}|+m\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{1\leq r\leq 10\}}.

Moreover, there exists numbers
T_{1}^{abcd}
and
S_{2}^{abc}
for
a,b,c,d=1,2,3
satisfying

\displaystyle\left|T_{1}^{abcd}\right|+\left|S_{2}^{abc}\right|\lesssim_{\max(\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})}\left(1+\eta^{-2}\max(m,m^{\prime})\right)d(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}^{\prime})

(4.15)

and such that

\displaystyle\left|\mathrm{Err}_{i}(\mathfrak{p})-\mathrm{Err}_{i}(\mathfrak{p}^{\prime})-T_{1}^{abcd}(1+r)^{-1}\partial_{a}\partial_{b}\widecheck{g}_{cd}-S_{2}^{abc}(1+r)^{-1}\partial_{a}\widecheck{\pi}_{bc}\right|

(4.16)

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim_{\max(\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})}\left((1+r)^{-2}\left(|\nabla\widecheck{g}|+|\widecheck{\pi}|\right)+(1+r)^{-3}|\widecheck{g}|\right)\left(1+\eta^{-2}\max(m,m^{\prime})\right)d(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\max\left(\left\|\breve{g}\right\|_{W^{2,\infty}},\left\|\breve{g}^{\prime}\right\|_{W^{2,\infty}},\left\|\breve{\pi}\right\|_{W^{1,\infty}},\left\|\breve{\pi}^{\prime}\right\|_{W^{1,\infty}}\right)\left(1+\eta^{-2}\max(m,m^{\prime})\right)d(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}^{\prime})\mathbbm{1}_{\{1\leq r\leq 10\}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\left(\max(m,m^{\prime})+|\widecheck{g}|+|\nabla\widecheck{g}|+|\widecheck{\pi}|\right)d(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}^{\prime})\mathbbm{1}_{\{1\leq r\leq 10\}},

for
\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}\in P
both satisfying (
4.11
) and
i=1,2
.

Proof.

Note that (
2.15
) and the assumption
m\leq 1
imply that

\displaystyle\left|e+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\gamma}1.

(4.17)

We start with the Hamiltonian constraint operator. We recall Definition
4.1
and expand:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{H}}(\bar{g}(\mathfrak{p}),\bar{\pi}(\mathfrak{p}))

\displaystyle={\mathcal{H}}(e+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e)+\widecheck{g}+\breve{g},\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}+\widecheck{\pi}+\breve{\pi})

\displaystyle={\mathcal{H}}(e+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}})+D{\mathcal{H}}[e+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}](\widecheck{g}+\breve{g},\widecheck{\pi}+\breve{\pi})

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}D^{2}{\mathcal{H}}[e+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}]((\widecheck{g}+\breve{g},\widecheck{\pi}+\breve{\pi}),(\widecheck{g}+\breve{g},\widecheck{\pi}+\breve{\pi}))

\displaystyle\quad+\mathrm{Err}{\mathcal{H}}[e+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}](\widecheck{g}+\breve{g},\widecheck{\pi}+\breve{\pi})

\displaystyle={\mathcal{H}}(e+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}})+A_{1}(\mathfrak{p})+A_{2}(\mathfrak{p})+A_{3}(\mathfrak{p}),

where
D{\mathcal{H}}
,
D^{2}{\mathcal{H}}
and
\mathrm{Err}{\mathcal{H}}
are as defined in Lemma
2.10
, and

\displaystyle A_{1}(\mathfrak{p})

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=D{\mathcal{H}}[e+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}](\widecheck{g}+\breve{g},\widecheck{\pi}+\breve{\pi}),

\displaystyle A_{2}(\mathfrak{p})

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\frac{1}{2}D^{2}{\mathcal{H}}[e+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}]((\widecheck{g}+\breve{g},\widecheck{\pi}+\breve{\pi}),(\widecheck{g}+\breve{g},\widecheck{\pi}+\breve{\pi})),

\displaystyle A_{3}(\mathfrak{p})

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\mathrm{Err}{\mathcal{H}}[e+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}](\widecheck{g}+\breve{g},\widecheck{\pi}+\breve{\pi}).

Thanks to (
2.3
) we have schematically

\displaystyle A_{1}(\mathfrak{p})-D{\mathcal{H}}[e,0]\left(\breve{g}\right)

\displaystyle=\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e)\nabla^{2}(\widecheck{g}+\breve{g})+(\widecheck{g}+\breve{g})\nabla^{2}(\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e))

(4.18)

\displaystyle\quad+(e+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e))^{2}\left(\nabla(\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e))\nabla(\widecheck{g}+\breve{g})+\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}(\widecheck{\pi}+\breve{\pi})\right)

\displaystyle\quad+(e+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e))(\widecheck{g}+\breve{g})\left((\nabla(\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e)))^{2}+(\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}})^{2}\right)

where we have used that
D{\mathcal{H}}[e,0]\left(\widecheck{g}\right)=0
since
\widecheck{g}
is TT (recall (
2.10
)). Thanks to (
2.5
) we have schematically

\displaystyle A_{2}(\mathfrak{p})-\frac{1}{2}D^{2}{\mathcal{H}}[e,0]\left(\left(\widecheck{g}+\breve{g},\widecheck{\pi}+\breve{\pi}\right),\left(\widecheck{g}+\breve{g},\widecheck{\pi}+\breve{\pi}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt=(e+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e))(\widecheck{g}+\breve{g})\left(\nabla(\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e))\nabla(\widecheck{g}+\breve{g})+\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}(\widecheck{\pi}+\breve{\pi})\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\quad+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e)\left((\nabla(\widecheck{g}+\breve{g}))^{2}+(\widecheck{\pi}+\breve{\pi})^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\quad+\left((\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}})^{2}+(\nabla(\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e)))^{2}\right)(\widecheck{g}+\breve{g})^{2}.

Moreover, using (
2.11
) and the fact that
\widecheck{g}
is TT we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}D^{2}{\mathcal{H}}[e,0]\left(\left(\widecheck{g}+\breve{g},\widecheck{\pi}+\breve{\pi}\right),\left(\widecheck{g}+\breve{g},\widecheck{\pi}+\breve{\pi}\right)\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}D^{2}{\mathcal{H}}[e,0]\left(\left(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi}\right),\left(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi}\right)\right)-\breve{g}^{ij}\Delta\widecheck{g}_{ij}

\displaystyle=\widecheck{g}\nabla^{2}\breve{g}+\breve{g}\nabla^{2}\breve{g}+\nabla\widecheck{g}\nabla\breve{g}+(\nabla\breve{g})^{2}+\widecheck{\pi}\breve{\pi}+(\breve{\pi})^{2},

so that we obtain

\displaystyle A_{2}(\mathfrak{p})-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}D^{2}{\mathcal{H}}[e,0]\left(\left(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi}\right),\left(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi}\right)\right)-\breve{g}^{ij}\Delta\widecheck{g}_{ij}

\displaystyle=(e+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e))(\widecheck{g}+\breve{g})\left(\nabla(\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e))\nabla(\widecheck{g}+\breve{g})+\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}(\widecheck{\pi}+\breve{\pi})\right)

(4.19)

\displaystyle\quad+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e)\left((\nabla(\widecheck{g}+\breve{g}))^{2}+(\widecheck{\pi}+\breve{\pi})^{2}\right)+\left((\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}})^{2}+(\nabla(\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e)))^{2}\right)(\widecheck{g}+\breve{g})^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\widecheck{g}\nabla^{2}\breve{g}+\breve{g}\nabla^{2}\breve{g}+\nabla\widecheck{g}\nabla\breve{g}+(\nabla\breve{g})^{2}+\widecheck{\pi}\breve{\pi}+(\breve{\pi})^{2}.

Finally, (
2.8
) gives

\displaystyle A_{3}(\mathfrak{p})

\displaystyle=(e+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e))(\widecheck{g}+\breve{g})(\nabla(\widecheck{g}+\breve{g}))^{2}

(4.20)

\displaystyle\quad+(\widecheck{g}+\breve{g})^{2}\left(\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}(\widecheck{\pi}+\breve{\pi})+\nabla(\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e))\nabla(\widecheck{g}+\breve{g})\right)

\displaystyle\quad+(\widecheck{g}+\breve{g})^{2}\left((\nabla(\widecheck{g}+\breve{g}))^{2}+(\widecheck{\pi}+\breve{\pi})^{2}\right).

Defining

\displaystyle\mathrm{Err}_{1}=\sum_{i=1}^{3}A_{i}(\mathfrak{p}),

and putting (
4.18
), (
4.19
) and (
4.20
) together we obtain (
4.12
) with the following schematic expression for the error term:

\displaystyle\mathrm{Err}_{1}(\mathfrak{p})

\displaystyle=(\widecheck{g}+\breve{g})\nabla^{2}(\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e))

\displaystyle\quad+(e+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e))^{2}\left(\nabla(\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e))\nabla(\widecheck{g}+\breve{g})+\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}(\widecheck{\pi}+\breve{\pi})\right)

\displaystyle\quad+(e+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e))(\widecheck{g}+\breve{g})\left(\left(\nabla(\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e)+\widecheck{g}+\breve{g})\right)^{2}+(\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}})^{2}+\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}(\widecheck{\pi}+\breve{\pi})\right)

\displaystyle\quad+(\widecheck{g}+\breve{g})^{2}\left((\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}+\widecheck{\pi}+\breve{\pi})^{2}+\left(\nabla(\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e)+\widecheck{g}+\breve{g})\right)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e)\left(\nabla^{2}(\widecheck{g}+\breve{g})+(\nabla(\widecheck{g}+\breve{g}))^{2}+(\widecheck{\pi}+\breve{\pi})^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\widecheck{g}\nabla^{2}\breve{g}+\breve{g}\nabla^{2}\breve{g}+\nabla\widecheck{g}\nabla\breve{g}+(\nabla\breve{g})^{2}+\widecheck{\pi}\breve{\pi}+(\breve{\pi})^{2}.

Using (
2.15
), (
4.11
), (
4.17
),
\left\|\widecheck{g}\right\|_{C^{2}_{-q-\delta}}+\left\|\widecheck{\pi}\right\|_{C^{1}_{-q-\delta-1}}\lesssim\varepsilon
(follows from Lemma
2.3
) and the fact that
\breve{g}
and
\breve{\pi}
are supported in
\{1\leq r\leq 10\}
, we first obtain the bound

\displaystyle\left|\mathrm{Err}_{1}(\mathfrak{p})\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon m}{(1+r)^{q+\delta+3}}+\frac{\varepsilon}{(1+r)^{q+\delta}}\left(|\nabla\widecheck{g}|^{2}+|\widecheck{\pi}|^{2}\right)+\left(\left\|\breve{g}\right\|_{W^{2,\infty}}^{2}+\left\|\breve{\pi}\right\|_{W^{1,\infty}}^{2}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{1\leq r\leq 10\}}

(4.21)

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\left\|\breve{g}\right\|_{W^{2,\infty}}+\left\|\breve{\pi}\right\|_{W^{1,\infty}}\right)\left(|\widecheck{g}|+|\nabla\widecheck{g}|+|\widecheck{\pi}|+m\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{1\leq r\leq 10\}}.

Using in addition the distance
d(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}^{\prime})
defined in (
4.1
) and (
4.2
) we also obtain

\displaystyle\left|\mathrm{Err}_{1}(\mathfrak{p})-\mathrm{Err}_{1}(\mathfrak{p}^{\prime})-T_{1}^{abcd}(1+r)^{-1}\partial_{a}\partial_{b}\widecheck{g}_{cd}\right|

(4.22)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\lesssim_{\max(\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})}\left((1+r)^{-2}\left(|\nabla\widecheck{g}|+|\widecheck{\pi}|\right)+(1+r)^{-3}|\widecheck{g}|\right)\left(1+\eta^{-2}\max(m,m^{\prime})\right)d(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\quad+\max\left(\left\|\breve{g}\right\|_{W^{2,\infty}},\left\|\breve{g}^{\prime}\right\|_{W^{2,\infty}}\right)\left(1+\eta^{-2}\max(m,m^{\prime})\right)d(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}^{\prime})\mathbbm{1}_{\{1\leq r\leq 10\}}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\quad+\left(\max(m,m^{\prime})+|\widecheck{g}|+|\nabla\widecheck{g}|+|\widecheck{\pi}|\right)d(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}^{\prime})\mathbbm{1}_{\{1\leq r\leq 10\}},

where the numbers
T_{1}^{abcd}
satisfy
\left|T_{1}^{abcd}\right|\lesssim\left(1+\eta^{-2}\max(m,m^{\prime})\right)d(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}^{\prime})
. The terms
T_{1}^{abcd}(1+r)^{-1}\partial_{a}\partial_{b}\widecheck{g}_{cd}
come from the terms
\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e)\nabla^{2}\widecheck{g}
in
\mathrm{Err}_{1}(\mathfrak{p})
, see Remark
4.5
for an explanation on why we need to isolate them.

We now look at the momentum constraint. We recall Definition
4.1
and expand with the notations of Lemma
2.10
:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{M}}(\bar{g}(\mathfrak{p}),\bar{\pi}(\mathfrak{p}))

\displaystyle={\mathcal{M}}(e+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e)+\widecheck{g}+\breve{g},\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}+\widecheck{\pi}+\breve{\pi})

\displaystyle={\mathcal{M}}(e+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}})+D{\mathcal{M}}[e+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}](\widecheck{g}+\breve{g},\widecheck{\pi}+\breve{\pi})

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}D^{2}{\mathcal{M}}[e+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}]((\widecheck{g}+\breve{g},\widecheck{\pi}+\breve{\pi}),(\widecheck{g}+\breve{g},\widecheck{\pi}+\breve{\pi}))

\displaystyle\quad+\mathrm{Err}{\mathcal{M}}[e+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}](\widecheck{g}+\breve{g},\widecheck{\pi}+\breve{\pi})

\displaystyle=\vcentcolon{\mathcal{M}}(e+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}})+B_{1}(\mathfrak{p})+B_{2}(\mathfrak{p})+B_{3}(\mathfrak{p}).

Thanks to (
2.4
) we have schematically

\displaystyle B_{1}(\mathfrak{p})-D{\mathcal{M}}[e,0](\breve{\pi})

\displaystyle=\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e)\nabla(\widecheck{\pi}+\breve{\pi})+(\widecheck{g}+\breve{g})\nabla(\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}})

(4.23)

\displaystyle\quad+(e+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e))^{2}\left(\nabla(\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e))(\widecheck{\pi}+\breve{\pi})+\nabla(\widecheck{g}+\breve{g})\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+(e+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e))\nabla(\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e))\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}(\widecheck{g}+\breve{g})

where we have used (
2.12
) and the fact that
\widecheck{\pi}
is TT to get
D{\mathcal{M}}[e,0](\widecheck{\pi})=0
. Thanks to (
2.7
) we have schematically

\displaystyle B_{2}(\mathfrak{p})-\frac{1}{2}D^{2}{\mathcal{M}}[e,0]((\widecheck{g}+\breve{g},\widecheck{\pi}+\breve{\pi}),(\widecheck{g}+\breve{g},\widecheck{\pi}+\breve{\pi}))

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt=\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e)(\widecheck{g}+\breve{g})\nabla(\widecheck{\pi}+\breve{\pi})

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\quad+(e+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e))(\widecheck{g}+\breve{g})\left((\widecheck{\pi}+\breve{\pi})\nabla(\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e))+\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}\nabla(\widecheck{g}+\breve{g})\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\quad+\nabla(\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e))\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}(\widecheck{g}+\breve{g})^{2}.

Moreover, using (
2.13
) we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}D^{2}{\mathcal{M}}[e,0]((\widecheck{g}+\breve{g},\widecheck{\pi}+\breve{\pi}),(\widecheck{g}+\breve{g},\widecheck{\pi}+\breve{\pi})_{i}-\frac{1}{2}D^{2}{\mathcal{M}}[e,0]((\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi}),(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi}))_{i}+\breve{g}^{k\ell}\partial_{k}\widecheck{\pi}_{\ell i}

\displaystyle\hskip 227.62204pt=\widecheck{g}\nabla\breve{\pi}+\widecheck{\pi}\nabla\breve{g}+\breve{\pi}\nabla\widecheck{g}+\breve{g}\nabla\breve{\pi}+\breve{\pi}\nabla\breve{g},

so that we obtain

\displaystyle B_{2}(\mathfrak{p})_{i}-\frac{1}{2}D^{2}{\mathcal{M}}[e,0]((\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi}),(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi}))_{i}+\breve{g}^{k\ell}\partial_{k}\widecheck{\pi}_{\ell i}

(4.24)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt=\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e)(\widecheck{g}+\breve{g})\nabla(\widecheck{\pi}+\breve{\pi})

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\quad+(e+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e))(\widecheck{g}+\breve{g})\left((\widecheck{\pi}+\breve{\pi})\nabla(\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e))+\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}\nabla(\widecheck{g}+\breve{g})\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\quad+\nabla(\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e))\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}(\widecheck{g}+\breve{g})^{2}+\widecheck{g}\nabla\breve{\pi}+\widecheck{\pi}\nabla\breve{g}+\breve{\pi}\nabla\widecheck{g}+\breve{g}\nabla\breve{\pi}+\breve{\pi}\nabla\breve{g}.

Finally, (
2.9
) implies

\displaystyle B_{3}(\mathfrak{p})

\displaystyle=(e+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e))(\widecheck{g}+\breve{g})(\widecheck{\pi}+\breve{\pi})\nabla(\widecheck{g}+\breve{g})

(4.25)

\displaystyle\quad+(\widecheck{g}+\breve{g})^{2}(\widecheck{\pi}+\breve{\pi})\nabla(\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e)+\widecheck{g}+\breve{g})

\displaystyle\quad+(\widecheck{g}+\breve{g})^{2}\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}\nabla(\widecheck{g}+\breve{g}).

Putting (
4.23
), (
4.24
) and (
4.25
) together we obtain (
4.13
) with the following expression for the error term

\displaystyle\mathrm{Err}_{2}(\mathfrak{p})

\displaystyle=\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e)\nabla\widecheck{\pi}+(\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e)+\breve{g})\nabla\breve{\pi}+(\widecheck{g}+\breve{g}+\nabla\breve{g})\nabla(\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}+\breve{\pi})

\displaystyle\quad+(e+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e))^{2}\left(\nabla(\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e))(\widecheck{\pi}+\breve{\pi})+\nabla(\widecheck{g}+\breve{g})\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e)(\widecheck{g}+\breve{g})\nabla(\widecheck{\pi}+\breve{\pi})+\widecheck{\pi}\nabla\breve{g}+\breve{\pi}\nabla\widecheck{g}

\displaystyle\quad+(e+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e))(\widecheck{g}+\breve{g})\nabla(\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e)+\widecheck{g}+\breve{g})(\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}+\widecheck{\pi}+\breve{\pi})

\displaystyle\quad+(\widecheck{g}+\breve{g})^{2}(\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}+\widecheck{\pi}+\breve{\pi})\nabla(\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e)+\widecheck{g}+\breve{g}).

As above this implies

\displaystyle\left|\mathrm{Err}_{2}(\mathfrak{p})\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon m}{(1+r)^{q+\delta+3}}+\frac{\varepsilon}{(1+r)^{q+\delta}}\left(|\nabla\widecheck{g}|^{2}+|\widecheck{\pi}|^{2}\right)+\left(\left\|\breve{g}\right\|_{W^{2,\infty}}^{2}+\left\|\breve{\pi}\right\|_{W^{1,\infty}}^{2}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{1\leq r\leq 10\}}

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\left\|\breve{g}\right\|_{W^{2,\infty}}+\left\|\breve{\pi}\right\|_{W^{1,\infty}}\right)\left(|\widecheck{g}|+|\nabla\widecheck{g}|+|\widecheck{\pi}|+m\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{1\leq r\leq 10\}},

which implies (
4.14
) together with (
4.21
). We also obtain

\displaystyle\left|\mathrm{Err}_{2}(\mathfrak{p})-\mathrm{Err}_{2}(\mathfrak{p}^{\prime})-S_{2}^{abc}(1+r)^{-1}\partial_{a}\widecheck{\pi}_{bc}\right|

(4.26)

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim_{\max(\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})}\left((1+r)^{-2}\left(|\nabla\widecheck{g}|+|\widecheck{\pi}|\right)+(1+r)^{-3}|\widecheck{g}|\right)\left(1+\eta^{-2}\max(m,m^{\prime})\right)d(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\max\left(\left\|\breve{g}\right\|_{W^{2,\infty}},\left\|\breve{g}^{\prime}\right\|_{W^{2,\infty}},\left\|\breve{\pi}\right\|_{W^{1,\infty}},\left\|\breve{\pi}^{\prime}\right\|_{W^{1,\infty}}\right)\left(1+\eta^{-2}\max(m,m^{\prime})\right)d(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}^{\prime})\mathbbm{1}_{\{1\leq r\leq 10\}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\left(\max(m,m^{\prime})+|\widecheck{g}|+|\nabla\widecheck{g}|+|\widecheck{\pi}|\right)d(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}^{\prime})\mathbbm{1}_{\{1\leq r\leq 10\}},

where the numbers
S_{2}^{abc}
satisfy
\left|S_{2}^{abc}\right|\lesssim\left(1+\eta^{-2}\max(m,m^{\prime})\right)d(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}^{\prime})
. The terms
S_{2}^{abc}(1+r)^{-1}\partial_{a}\widecheck{\pi}_{bc}
comes from the terms
\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e)\nabla\widecheck{\pi}
in
\mathrm{Err}_{2}(\mathfrak{p})
, see Remark
4.5
for an explanation on why we need to isolate them. Together with (
4.22
), (
4.26
) implies (
4.16
) and concludes the proof of the proposition.
∎

Remark 4.5
.

Some of the terms involving
\nabla^{2}\widecheck{g}
and
\nabla\widecheck{\pi}
have been isolated in (
4.12
), (
4.13
) and (
4.16
) so that, after integration by parts, they can be estimated with the smallness constant
\eta
instead of
\varepsilon
(a similar remark applies for the terms of the form
\nabla^{2}\widecheck{g}
in (
4.28
) and (
4.29
) in the next proposition). This will be used in the proofs of Lemmas
4.13
and
4.18
.

Proposition 4.6
.

Let
\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)\in H^{2}_{-q-\delta}\times H^{2}_{-q-\delta}
and
\mathfrak{p}\in P
satisfying

\displaystyle m+\left\|\breve{g}\right\|_{W^{2,\infty}}+\left\|\breve{\pi}\right\|_{W^{1,\infty}}+\left\|\widecheck{u}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq\frac{1}{2}.

(4.27)

For
{\mathcal{O}}={\mathcal{H}},{\mathcal{M}}
, there exist numbers
U_{\mathcal{O}}^{abcd}
and
V_{\mathcal{O}}^{abcd}
for
a,b,c,d=1,2,3
satisfying

\displaystyle\left|U_{\mathcal{O}}^{abcd}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\gamma}1,

\displaystyle\left|V_{\mathcal{O}}^{abcd}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\max(\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})}1,

and such that

\displaystyle\left|{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathcal{O}}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)-U_{\mathcal{O}}^{abcd}\widecheck{u}\partial_{a}\partial_{b}\widecheck{g}_{cd}\right|

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim_{\gamma}\left|\nabla\widecheck{X}\right|^{2}+\left|\nabla\widecheck{u}\nabla\widecheck{X}\right|+\left(\frac{m}{1+r}+|\widecheck{g}|\right)\left|\nabla^{2}\left(\widecheck{u}+\widecheck{X}\right)\right|

(4.28)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\left(\frac{m}{(1+r)^{2}}+|\nabla\widecheck{g}|+|\widecheck{\pi}|\right)\left|\nabla\left(\widecheck{X}+\widecheck{u}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\left(\frac{m}{(1+r)^{3}}+\left(\frac{m}{(1+r)^{2}}+|\nabla\widecheck{g}|+|\widecheck{\pi}|\right)^{2}\right)|\widecheck{u}|

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\left(\left\|\breve{g}\right\|_{W^{2,\infty}}+\left\|\breve{\pi}\right\|_{W^{1,\infty}}\right)\left(\left|\nabla^{2}\widecheck{X}\right|+\left|\nabla\widecheck{X}\right|^{2}+\left|\nabla\widecheck{X}\right|+|\nabla^{\leq 2}\widecheck{u}|\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{1\leq r\leq 10\}},

and

\displaystyle\left|{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathcal{O}}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)-{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathcal{O}}\left(\mathfrak{p}^{\prime},\widecheck{u}^{\prime},\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right)-V_{\mathcal{O}}^{abcd}(\widecheck{u}-\widecheck{u}^{\prime})\partial_{a}\partial_{b}\widecheck{g}_{cd}\right|

(4.29)

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim_{\max(\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})}\left|\left(\nabla\widecheck{X}\right)^{2}-\left(\nabla\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right)^{2}\right|+\left|\nabla\widecheck{u}\nabla\widecheck{X}-\nabla\widecheck{u}^{\prime}\nabla\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right|

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\left(1+\eta^{-2}\max(m,m^{\prime})\right)\left|\nabla^{2}\left(\widecheck{u}+\widecheck{X}+\widecheck{u}^{\prime}+\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right)\right|\frac{d(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}^{\prime})}{1+r}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\left(1+\eta^{-2}\max(m,m^{\prime})\right)\left|\nabla\left(\widecheck{u}+\widecheck{X}+\widecheck{u}^{\prime}+\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right)\right|\frac{d(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}^{\prime})}{(1+r)^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\left(1+\eta^{-2}\max(m,m^{\prime})\right)|\widecheck{u}+\widecheck{u}^{\prime}|\frac{d(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}^{\prime})}{(1+r)^{3}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\left(\frac{m+m^{\prime}}{1+r}+|\widecheck{g}|\right)\left|\nabla^{2}\left(\widecheck{u}-\widecheck{u}^{\prime}+\widecheck{X}-\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\left(\frac{m+m^{\prime}}{(1+r)^{2}}+|\nabla\widecheck{g}|+|\widecheck{\pi}|\right)\left|\nabla\left(\widecheck{u}-\widecheck{u}^{\prime}+\widecheck{X}-\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\left(\frac{m+m^{\prime}}{(1+r)^{3}}+\left(\frac{m+m^{\prime}}{(1+r)^{2}}+|\nabla\widecheck{g}|+|\widecheck{\pi}|\right)^{2}\right)|\widecheck{u}-\widecheck{u}^{\prime}|

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\max\left(\left\|\breve{g}\right\|_{W^{2,\infty}},\left\|\breve{\pi}\right\|_{W^{1,\infty}},\left\|\breve{g}^{\prime}\right\|_{W^{2,\infty}},\left\|\breve{\pi}^{\prime}\right\|_{W^{1,\infty}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\quad\times\left|\nabla^{\leq 2}\left(\widecheck{u}-\widecheck{u}^{\prime}+\widecheck{X}-\widecheck{X^{\prime}}\right)+\left(\nabla\widecheck{X}\right)^{2}-\left(\nabla\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right)^{2}\right|\mathbbm{1}_{\{1\leq r\leq 10\}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\left|\nabla^{\leq 2}\left(\widecheck{u}+\widecheck{u}^{\prime}+\widecheck{X}+\widecheck{X^{\prime}}\right)+\left(\nabla\widecheck{X}\right)^{2}+\left(\nabla\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right)^{2}\right|d(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}^{\prime})\mathbbm{1}_{\{1\leq r\leq 10\}},

where
\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),\left(\widecheck{u}^{\prime},\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right)\in\left(H^{2}_{-q-\delta}\right)^{4}
and
\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}\in P
satisfy (
4.27
).

Proof.

We start with
{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathcal{H}}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)
, whose expression is given in (
4.5
). Schematically we obtain

\displaystyle{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathcal{H}}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)

\displaystyle=(1+\widecheck{u})^{-3}\bar{g}(\mathfrak{p})^{2}\left(\left(\nabla\widecheck{X}\right)^{2}+\bar{\pi}(\mathfrak{p})\nabla\widecheck{X}\right)+\left((1+\widecheck{u})^{-3}-1\right)\left(\bar{g}(\mathfrak{p})\bar{\pi}(\mathfrak{p})\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\widecheck{u}R(\bar{g}(\mathfrak{p}))+(\bar{g}(\mathfrak{p})-e)\nabla^{2}\widecheck{u}+\bar{g}(\mathfrak{p})^{2}\nabla\bar{g}(\mathfrak{p})\nabla\widecheck{u}.

From (
2.15
), (
4.27
), (
4.17
) and
\left|\left((1+\widecheck{u})^{-3}-1\right)\right|\lesssim|\widecheck{u}|
we obtain

\displaystyle\left|{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathcal{H}}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)-U_{\mathcal{H}}^{abcd}\widecheck{u}\partial_{a}\partial_{b}\widecheck{g}_{cd}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\gamma}\left|\nabla\widecheck{X}\right|^{2}+\left(\frac{m}{(1+r)^{2}}+|\widecheck{\pi}|\right)\left|\nabla\left(\widecheck{X}+\widecheck{u}\right)\right|+|\widecheck{u}|\left(\frac{m}{(1+r)^{2}}+|\nabla\widecheck{g}|+|\widecheck{\pi}|\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+|\widecheck{u}|\frac{m}{(1+r)^{3}}+\left(\frac{m}{1+r}+|\widecheck{g}|\right)|\nabla^{2}\widecheck{u}|+|\nabla\widecheck{g}||\nabla\widecheck{u}|

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\left\|\breve{g}\right\|_{W^{2,\infty}}+\left\|\breve{\pi}\right\|_{W^{1,\infty}}\right)\left(\left|\nabla\widecheck{X}\right|^{2}+|\nabla\widecheck{X}|+|\nabla^{\leq 2}\widecheck{u}|\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{1\leq r\leq 10\}},

where the terms of the form
\widecheck{u}\nabla^{2}\widecheck{g}
that we isolated come from
\widecheck{u}R(\bar{g}(\mathfrak{p}))
in
{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathcal{H}}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)
.
Moreover, using (
4.2
) we also obtain

\displaystyle\left|{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathcal{H}}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)-{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathcal{H}}\left(\mathfrak{p}^{\prime},\widecheck{u}^{\prime},\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right)-V_{\mathcal{H}}^{abcd}(\widecheck{u}-\widecheck{u}^{\prime})\partial_{a}\partial_{b}\widecheck{g}_{cd}\right|

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim_{\max(\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})}\left|\left(\nabla\widecheck{X}\right)^{2}-\left(\nabla\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right)^{2}\right|

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\left(1+\eta^{-2}\max(m,m^{\prime})\right)\left|\nabla^{2}\left(\widecheck{u}+\widecheck{X}+\widecheck{u}^{\prime}+\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right)\right|\frac{d(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}^{\prime})}{1+r}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\left(\frac{m+m^{\prime}}{1+r}+|\widecheck{g}|\right)\left|\nabla^{2}(\widecheck{u}-\widecheck{u}^{\prime})\right|

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\left(1+\eta^{-2}\max(m,m^{\prime})\right)\left|\nabla\left(\widecheck{u}+\widecheck{X}+\widecheck{u}^{\prime}+\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right)\right|\frac{d(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}^{\prime})}{(1+r)^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\left(\frac{m+m^{\prime}}{(1+r)^{2}}+|\nabla\widecheck{g}|+|\widecheck{\pi}|\right)\left|\nabla\left(\widecheck{u}-\widecheck{u}^{\prime}+\widecheck{X}-\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\left(1+\eta^{-2}\max(m,m^{\prime})\right)|\widecheck{u}+\widecheck{u}^{\prime}|\frac{d(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}^{\prime})}{(1+r)^{3}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\left(\frac{m+m^{\prime}}{(1+r)^{3}}+\left(\frac{m+m^{\prime}}{(1+r)^{2}}+|\nabla\widecheck{g}|+|\widecheck{\pi}|\right)^{2}\right)|\widecheck{u}-\widecheck{u}^{\prime}|

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\max\left(\left\|\breve{g}\right\|_{W^{2,\infty}},\left\|\breve{\pi}\right\|_{W^{1,\infty}},\left\|\breve{g}^{\prime}\right\|_{W^{2,\infty}},\left\|\breve{\pi}^{\prime}\right\|_{W^{1,\infty}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\quad\times\left|\nabla^{\leq 2}(\widecheck{u}-\widecheck{u}^{\prime})+\nabla\left(\widecheck{X}-\widecheck{X^{\prime}}\right)+\left(\nabla\widecheck{X}\right)^{2}-\left(\nabla\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right)^{2}\right|\mathbbm{1}_{\{1\leq r\leq 10\}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\left|\nabla^{\leq 2}(\widecheck{u}+\widecheck{u}^{\prime})+\nabla\left(\widecheck{X}+\widecheck{X^{\prime}}\right)+\left(\nabla\widecheck{X}\right)^{2}+\left(\nabla\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right)^{2}\right|d(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}^{\prime})\mathbbm{1}_{\{1\leq r\leq 10\}}.

We deal with
{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathcal{M}}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)
in a similar way, relying on (
4.6
) which implies the following schematic expression

\displaystyle{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathcal{M}}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)

\displaystyle=(1+\widecheck{u})^{-1}\bar{g}(\mathfrak{p})\nabla\widecheck{u}\left(\bar{\pi}(\mathfrak{p})+\nabla\widecheck{X}\right)+(\bar{g}(\mathfrak{p})-e)\nabla^{2}\widecheck{X}+\bar{g}(\mathfrak{p})^{2}\nabla\bar{g}(\mathfrak{p})\nabla\widecheck{X}.

This concludes the proof of the proposition.
∎

4.3
Projections of
A\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)
and
B\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)

In this section, we give two lemmas computing the projections appearing on the RHS of the orthogonality conditions (
4.7
)-(
4.10
). Note that we still assume that the parameter
\mathfrak{p}\in P
satisfies (
4.27
) so that the results of Propositions
4.4
and
4.6
hold.

Lemma 4.7
.

For
-1\leq\ell\leq 1
we have

\displaystyle\left\langle A\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),w_{1,0}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=16\pi\gamma m-\eta^{2}+{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{H}},1}(\vec{p})

(4.30)

\displaystyle\quad+\left\langle\breve{g}^{ij}\Delta\widecheck{g}_{ij}+\mathrm{Err}_{1}(\mathfrak{p})+{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathcal{H}}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),w_{1,0}\right\rangle,

\displaystyle\left\langle A\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),w_{2,\ell}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\sqrt{3}my^{\ell^{\prime}}+Q^{A}_{2,\ell}(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi})+\sqrt{3}{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{H}},x^{\ell^{\prime}}}(\vec{p})

(4.31)

\displaystyle\quad+\left\langle\breve{g}^{ij}\Delta\widecheck{g}_{ij}+\mathrm{Err}_{1}(\mathfrak{p})+{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathcal{H}}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),w_{2,\ell}\right\rangle,

where
\ell^{\prime}
is such that
6
6

6

In fact, we have
w_{2,-1}=\sqrt{3}x^{1}
,
w_{2,0}=\sqrt{3}x^{3}
and
w_{2,1}=\sqrt{3}x^{2}
.

w_{2,\ell}=\sqrt{3}x^{\ell^{\prime}}
, and

\displaystyle|Q^{A}_{2,\ell}(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi})|\lesssim\eta^{2(1-\theta)}\varepsilon^{2\theta},

with

\displaystyle\theta\vcentcolon=\frac{1}{2q+2\delta-1}.

For
3\leq j\leq q
and
-(j-1)\leq\ell\leq j-1
we have

\displaystyle\left\langle A\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),w_{j,\ell}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle\breve{g},D{\mathcal{H}}[e,0]^{*}(w_{j,\ell})\right\rangle+\left\langle{\mathcal{H}}\left(e+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}\right),w_{j,\ell}\right\rangle

(4.32)

\displaystyle\quad+Q^{A}_{j,\ell}(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi})+\left\langle\breve{g}^{ij}\Delta\widecheck{g}_{ij}+\mathrm{Err}_{1}(\mathfrak{p})+{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathcal{H}}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),w_{j,\ell}\right\rangle,

where

\displaystyle|Q^{A}_{j,\ell}(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi})|\lesssim\eta\varepsilon.

Proof.

Thanks to (
4.12
) we have

\displaystyle\left\langle A\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),w_{1,0}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle{\mathcal{H}}\left(e+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}\right),w_{1,0}\right\rangle+\left\langle D{\mathcal{H}}[e,0]\left(\breve{g}\right),w_{1,0}\right\rangle

(4.33)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\left\langle D^{2}{\mathcal{H}}[e,0]\left(\left(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi}\right),\left(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi}\right)\right),w_{1,0}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\left\langle\breve{g}^{ij}\Delta\widecheck{g}_{ij}+\mathrm{Err}_{1}(\mathfrak{p})+{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathcal{H}}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),w_{1,0}\right\rangle.

For the first term in (
4.33
), we use (
2.19
). The second term vanishes since
w_{1,0}
belongs to the kernel of
D{\mathcal{H}}[e,0]^{*}
(see Proposition
2.13
). For the third term we use (
2.11
), the fact that
\widecheck{g}
and
\widecheck{\pi}
are TT and integrate by parts to obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left\langle D^{2}{\mathcal{H}}[e,0]\left(\left(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi}\right),\left(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi}\right)\right),w_{1,0}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\left(\widecheck{g}^{ij}\Delta\widecheck{g}_{ij}+\frac{3}{4}|\nabla\widecheck{g}|^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\partial^{b}\widecheck{g}^{jk}\partial_{j}\widecheck{g}_{kb}-|\widecheck{\pi}|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{4}\left\|\nabla\widecheck{g}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}-\left\|\widecheck{\pi}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=-\eta^{2},

where we recall that
w_{1,0}=1
. This concludes the proof of (
4.30
).

We now turn to the proof of (
4.31
). We again use (
4.12
) to obtain

\displaystyle\left\langle A\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),w_{2,\ell}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle{\mathcal{H}}\left(e+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}\right),w_{2,\ell}\right\rangle+\left\langle D{\mathcal{H}}[e,0]\left(\breve{g}\right),w_{2,\ell}\right\rangle

(4.34)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\left\langle D^{2}{\mathcal{H}}[e,0]\left(\left(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi}\right),\left(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi}\right)\right),w_{2,\ell}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\left\langle\breve{g}^{ij}\Delta\widecheck{g}_{ij}+\mathrm{Err}_{1}(\mathfrak{p})+{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathcal{H}}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),w_{2,\ell}\right\rangle.

For the first term in (
4.34
), we use (
2.21
). The second term vanishes since
w_{2,\ell}
belongs to the kernel of
D{\mathcal{H}}[e,0]^{*}
(see Proposition
2.13
). For the third term, we rename it
Q^{A}_{2,\ell}(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi})
and use (
2.11
) and the fact that
\widecheck{g}
is TT:

\displaystyle\left|Q^{A}_{2,\ell}(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi})\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}r|\nabla\widecheck{g}|^{2}+\left|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}x^{\ell^{\prime}}\widecheck{g}^{ij}\Delta\widecheck{g}_{ij}\right|+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}r|\widecheck{\pi}|^{2},

(4.35)

where we also used
|w_{2,\ell}|\lesssim r
. We estimate each integral on the RHS of (
4.35
).

•

For the first integral, we apply Hölder’s inequality with well-chosen exponents

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}r|\nabla\widecheck{g}|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|(\nabla\widecheck{g})^{2-2\theta}\right\|_{L^{\frac{1}{1-\theta}}}\left\|(1+r)(\nabla\widecheck{g})^{2\theta}\right\|_{L^{\frac{1}{\theta}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\nabla\widecheck{g}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2(1-\theta)}\left\|(1+r)^{\frac{1}{2\theta}}\nabla\widecheck{g}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2\theta}

\displaystyle\lesssim\eta^{2(1-\theta)}\varepsilon^{2\theta},

where we recall that
\theta=\frac{1}{2q+2\delta-1}
.

•

For the second integral, we integrate by parts

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}x^{\ell^{\prime}}\widecheck{g}^{ij}\Delta\widecheck{g}_{ij}

\displaystyle=-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\partial^{k}(x^{\ell^{\prime}}\widecheck{g}^{ij})\partial_{k}\widecheck{g}_{ij}

\displaystyle=-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}x^{\ell^{\prime}}|\nabla\widecheck{g}|^{2}-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\widecheck{g}^{ij}\partial_{\ell^{\prime}}\widecheck{g}_{ij}.

By integration by parts again, we see that
\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\widecheck{g}^{ij}\partial_{\ell^{\prime}}\widecheck{g}_{ij}=\frac{1}{2}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\partial_{\ell^{\prime}}|\widecheck{g}|^{2}=0
. Therefore we have

\displaystyle\left|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}x^{\ell^{\prime}}\widecheck{g}^{ij}\Delta\widecheck{g}_{ij}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}r|\nabla\widecheck{g}|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\eta^{2(1-\theta)}\varepsilon^{2\theta},

where we used the estimate of the first integral.

•

The third integral on the RHS of (
4.35
) is estimated in the same manner as the first using the fact that
\widecheck{\pi}
satisfies the same
L^{2}
bounds as
\nabla\widecheck{g}
up to a constant factor.

We have thus proved that

\displaystyle\left|Q^{A}_{2,\ell}(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi})\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\eta^{2(1-\theta)}\varepsilon^{2\theta}.

This concludes the proof of (
4.31
).

Finally, we prove (
4.32
). This proceeds in a similar manner to before. From (
4.12
) we obtain

\displaystyle\left\langle A\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),w_{j,\ell}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle{\mathcal{H}}\left(e+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}\right),w_{j,\ell}\right\rangle+\left\langle D{\mathcal{H}}[e,0]\left(\breve{g}\right),w_{j,\ell}\right\rangle

(4.36)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\left\langle D^{2}{\mathcal{H}}[e,0]\left(\left(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi}\right),\left(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi}\right)\right),w_{j,\ell}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\left\langle\breve{g}^{ij}\Delta\widecheck{g}_{ij}+\mathrm{Err}_{1}(\mathfrak{p})+{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathcal{H}}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),w_{j,\ell}\right\rangle.

We leave the first term as it is. The second term can be rewritten

\displaystyle\left\langle D{\mathcal{H}}[e,0]\left(\breve{g}\right),w_{j,\ell}\right\rangle=\left\langle\breve{g},D{\mathcal{H}}[e,0]^{*}(w_{j,\ell})\right\rangle.

For the third term in (
4.36
), we rename it
Q^{A}_{j,\ell}(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi})
and use (
2.11
),
|w_{j,\ell}|\lesssim r^{j-1}
and integrate by parts the terms of the form
\widecheck{g}\nabla^{2}\widecheck{g}
:

\displaystyle\left|Q^{A}_{j,\ell}(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi})\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}r^{j-1}\left(|\nabla\widecheck{g}|^{2}+|\widecheck{\pi}|^{2}\right)+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}r^{j-2}|\widecheck{g}||\nabla\widecheck{g}|.

(4.37)

We estimate each integral on the RHS of (
4.37
).

•

For the first integral, we apply Hölder’s inequality with well-chosen exponents

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}r^{j-1}|\nabla\widecheck{g}|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|(\nabla\widecheck{g})^{2-2(j-1)\theta}\right\|_{L^{\frac{1}{1-(j-1)\theta}}}\left\|(1+r)^{j-1}(\nabla\widecheck{g})^{2(j-1)\theta}\right\|_{L^{\frac{1}{(j-1)\theta}}}

(4.38)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\nabla\widecheck{g}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2(1-(j-1)\theta)}\left\|(1+r)^{\frac{1}{2\theta}}\nabla\widecheck{g}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2(j-1)\theta}

\displaystyle\lesssim\eta^{2(1-(j-1)\theta)}\varepsilon^{2(j-1)\theta}

\displaystyle\lesssim\eta\varepsilon,

where we used
\eta\lesssim\varepsilon
and
2(j-1)\theta<1
since
j\leq q
and
\delta>0
. Since
\widecheck{\pi}
and
\nabla\widecheck{g}
satisfy the same estimates we also obtain

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}r^{j-1}|\widecheck{\pi}|^{2}\lesssim\eta\varepsilon.

•

For the second integral in (
4.37
) we first use Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and Hardy’s inequality
\left\|r^{-1}\widecheck{g}\right\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\left\|\nabla\widecheck{g}\right\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\eta
to get

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}r^{j-2}|\widecheck{g}||\nabla\widecheck{g}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\eta\left(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}(1+r)^{2(j-1)}|\nabla\widecheck{g}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

We estimate the integral
\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}(1+r)^{2(j-1)}|\nabla\widecheck{g}|^{2}
as above, with a
(1+r)^{2(j-1)}
weight instead of a
(1+r)^{j-1}
weight. Using
2(j-1)\theta<1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}(1+r)^{2(j-1)}|\nabla\widecheck{g}|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\eta^{2(1-2(j-1)\theta)}\varepsilon^{4(j-1)\theta}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon^{2}

and thus

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}r^{j-2}|\widecheck{g}||\nabla\widecheck{g}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\eta\varepsilon.

We have proved that

\displaystyle\left|Q^{A}_{j,\ell}(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi})\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\eta\varepsilon.

This concludes the proof of (
4.32
).
∎

Lemma 4.8
.

For
k,a,b=1,2,3
we have

\displaystyle\left\langle B\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),W_{1,0,k}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=8\pi\gamma mv^{k}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\widecheck{\pi}^{ij}\partial_{k}\widecheck{g}_{ij}-{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{M}},W_{1,0,k}}(\vec{p})

(4.39)

\displaystyle\quad+\left\langle\breve{g}^{k\ell}\partial_{k}\widecheck{\pi}_{\ell i}-\mathrm{Err}_{2}(\mathfrak{p})_{i}+{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathcal{M}}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)_{i},W_{1,0,k}^{i}\right\rangle,

\displaystyle\left\langle B\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),\Omega^{-}_{ab}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-8\pi ma^{k}+Q^{B}_{ab}(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi})-{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{M}},\Omega^{-}_{ab}}(\vec{p})

(4.40)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\left\langle\breve{g}^{k\ell}\partial_{k}\widecheck{\pi}_{\ell i}-\mathrm{Err}_{2}(\mathfrak{p})_{i}+{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathcal{M}}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)_{i},(\Omega^{-}_{ab})^{i}\right\rangle,

where
k
is such that
\in_{kab}=1
, and

\displaystyle|Q^{B}_{ab}(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi})|\lesssim\eta^{2(1-\theta)}\varepsilon^{2\theta}.

For
Z\in\mathcal{Z}_{q}
we have

\displaystyle\left\langle B\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),Z\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-\left\langle\breve{\pi},D{\mathcal{M}}[e,0]^{*}(Z)\right\rangle-\left\langle{\mathcal{M}}\left(e+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}\right),Z\right\rangle

(4.41)

\displaystyle\quad+Q^{B}_{Z}(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi})+\left\langle\breve{g}^{k\ell}\partial_{k}\widecheck{\pi}_{\ell i}-\mathrm{Err}_{2}(\mathfrak{p})_{i}+{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathcal{M}}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)_{i},Z^{i}\right\rangle,

where

\displaystyle|Q^{B}_{Z}(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi})|\lesssim\eta\varepsilon.

Proof.

We start with the proof of (
4.39
). Thanks to (
4.13
) we have

\displaystyle\left\langle B\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),W_{1,0,k}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-\left\langle{\mathcal{M}}\left(e+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}\right),W_{1,0,k}\right\rangle

(4.42)

\displaystyle\quad-\left\langle D{\mathcal{M}}[e,0](\breve{\pi}),W_{1,0,k}\right\rangle-\frac{1}{2}\left\langle D^{2}{\mathcal{M}}[e,0]((\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi}),(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi})),W_{1,0,k}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\left\langle\breve{g}^{k\ell}\partial_{k}\widecheck{\pi}_{\ell i}-\mathrm{Err}_{2}(\mathfrak{p})_{i}+{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathcal{M}}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)_{i},W_{1,0,k}^{i}\right\rangle.

For the first term on the RHS of (
4.42
) we use (
2.20
). The second term vanishes since
W_{1,0,k}
belongs to the kernel of
D{\mathcal{M}}[e,0]^{*}
(see Proposition
2.13
). For the third term, we use (
2.13
), integrate by parts and use the fact that
\widecheck{g}
is TT:

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\left\langle D^{2}{\mathcal{M}}[e,0]((\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi}),(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi})),W_{1,0,k}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\widecheck{\pi}^{ij}\partial_{k}\widecheck{g}_{ij}.

This concludes the proof of (
4.39
).

We now turn to the proof of (
4.40
), we again use (
4.13
) to obtain

\displaystyle\left\langle B\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),\Omega^{-}_{ab}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-\left\langle{\mathcal{M}}\left(e+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}\right),\Omega^{-}_{ab}\right\rangle-\left\langle D{\mathcal{M}}[e,0](\breve{\pi}),\Omega^{-}_{ab}\right\rangle

(4.43)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2}\left\langle D^{2}{\mathcal{M}}[e,0]((\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi}),(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi})),\Omega^{-}_{ab}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\left\langle\breve{g}^{k\ell}\partial_{k}\widecheck{\pi}_{\ell i}-\mathrm{Err}_{2}(\mathfrak{p})_{i}+{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathcal{M}}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)_{i},(\Omega^{-}_{ab})^{i}\right\rangle.

For the first term in (
4.43
), we use (
2.22
). The second term vanishes since
\Omega^{-}_{ab}
belongs to the kernel of
D{\mathcal{M}}[e,0]^{*}
(see Proposition
2.13
). For the third term, we rename it
Q^{B}_{ab}(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi})
, use (
2.13
), integrate by parts and use the fact that
\widecheck{g}
is TT:

\displaystyle\left|Q^{B}_{ab}(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi})\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}r|\widecheck{\pi}|^{2}+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}r|\nabla\widecheck{g}|^{2}+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|\widecheck{g}||\widecheck{\pi}|.

(4.44)

The first two integrals on the RHS of (
4.44
) are estimated as in the proof of (
4.31
):

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}r|\widecheck{\pi}|^{2}+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}r|\nabla\widecheck{g}|^{2}\lesssim\eta^{2(1-\theta)}\varepsilon^{2\theta}.

For the third integral on the RHS of (
4.44
), we first use Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and Hardy’s inequality
\left\|r^{-1}\widecheck{g}\right\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\left\|\nabla\widecheck{g}\right\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\eta
to get

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|\widecheck{g}||\widecheck{\pi}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\eta\left(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}(1+r)^{2}|\widecheck{\pi}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

We estimate the integral
\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}(1+r)^{2}|\widecheck{\pi}|^{2}
as in the proof of (
4.31
), with now a
(1+r)^{2}
weight instead of a
1+r
weight. We obtain

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}(1+r)^{2}|\widecheck{\pi}|^{2}\lesssim\eta^{2\left(1-2\theta\right)}\varepsilon^{4\theta},

and thus

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|\widecheck{g}||\widecheck{\pi}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\eta^{2(1-\theta)}\varepsilon^{2\theta}.

We have proved that

\displaystyle\left|Q^{B}_{ab}(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi})\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\eta^{2(1-\theta)}\varepsilon^{2\theta}.

This concludes the proof of (
4.40
).

Finally we prove (
4.41
). Thanks to (
4.12
) we have

\displaystyle\left\langle B\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),Z\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-\left\langle{\mathcal{M}}\left(e+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}\right),Z\right\rangle-\left\langle D{\mathcal{M}}[e,0](\breve{\pi}),Z\right\rangle

(4.45)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2}\left\langle D^{2}{\mathcal{M}}[e,0]((\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi}),(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi})),Z\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\left\langle\breve{g}^{k\ell}\partial_{k}\widecheck{\pi}_{\ell i}-\mathrm{Err}_{2}(\mathfrak{p})_{i}+{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathcal{M}}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)_{i},Z^{i}\right\rangle.

We leave the first term as it is. The second term can be rewritten as

\displaystyle-\left\langle D{\mathcal{M}}[e,0](\breve{\pi}),Z\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-\left\langle\breve{\pi},D{\mathcal{M}}[e,0]^{*}(Z)\right\rangle.

For the third term in (
4.45
), we rename it
Q^{B}_{Z}(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi})
and proceed as we did for the third term in (
4.36
), since for each
Z\in\mathcal{Z}_{q}
there exists
j\leq q
such that
|Z|\lesssim r^{j-1}
. We obtain

\displaystyle\left|Q^{B}_{Z}(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi})\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\eta\varepsilon.

This concludes the proof of (
4.41
).
∎

Remark 4.9
.

As Lemmas
4.7
and
4.8
show, if
q=1
we only need the mass
m
and the boost velocity
v
. Similarly, if
q=2
, we need the full black hole parameter
\vec{p}=(m,y,a,v)
and the corrector
\breve{\pi}
but we don’t need the corrector
\breve{g}
.

4.4
Solving for
\mathfrak{p}\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)

We now solve for
\mathfrak{p}\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)
satisfying the orthogonality conditions (
4.7
)-(
4.10
), assuming that the scalar function
\widecheck{u}
and vector field
\widecheck{X}
satisfy

\displaystyle\left\|\widecheck{u}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}+\left\|\widecheck{X}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}\leq C_{0}\eta\varepsilon,

(4.46)

for some
C_{0}>0
large enough and to be chosen later. The constant
\varepsilon
will then be chosen small compared to
C_{0}^{-1}
, so that the control of
\widecheck{u}
in (
4.46
) is consistent with (
4.27
).

4.4.1
The parameter map

According to Lemmas
4.7
and
4.8
, the orthogonality conditions (
4.7
)-(
4.10
) are equivalent to the following non-linear system for the parameter
\mathfrak{p}
:

\left\{\begin{aligned} 16\pi\gamma m&=\eta^{2}+F_{1}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),\\
16\pi\gamma mv^{k}&=-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\widecheck{\pi}^{ij}\partial_{k}\widecheck{g}_{ij}+F_{2,k}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),\\
my^{\ell^{\prime}}&=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}Q^{A}_{2,\ell}(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi})+\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}F_{3,\ell^{\prime}}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),\\
ma^{k}&=\frac{1}{8\pi}Q^{B}_{ab}(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi})+\frac{1}{8\pi}F_{4,k}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),\\
\left\langle\breve{g},D{\mathcal{H}}[e,0]^{*}(w_{j,\ell})\right\rangle&=-\left\langle{\mathcal{H}}\left(e+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}\right),w_{j,\ell}\right\rangle\\
&\quad-Q^{A}_{j,\ell}(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi})+F_{5,j,\ell}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),\\
\left\langle\breve{\pi},D{\mathcal{M}}[e,0]^{*}(Z)\right\rangle&=-\left\langle{\mathcal{M}}\left(e+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}\right),Z\right\rangle\\
&\quad+Q^{B}_{Z}(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi})+F_{6,Z}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),\end{aligned}\right.

(4.47)

where the remainders
F_{i}
are given by

\displaystyle F_{1}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)

\displaystyle=-{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{H}},1}(\vec{p})-\left\langle\breve{g}^{ij}\Delta\widecheck{g}_{ij}+\mathrm{Err}_{1}(\mathfrak{p})+{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathcal{H}}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),w_{1,0}\right\rangle,

\displaystyle F_{2,k}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)

\displaystyle={\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{M}},W_{1,0,k}}(\vec{p})-\left\langle\breve{g}^{k\ell}\partial_{k}\widecheck{\pi}_{\ell i}-\mathrm{Err}_{2}(\mathfrak{p})_{i}+{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathcal{M}}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)_{i},W_{1,0,k}^{i}\right\rangle,

\displaystyle F_{3,\ell^{\prime}}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)

\displaystyle=-{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{H}},x^{\ell^{\prime}}}(\vec{p})-\left\langle\breve{g}^{ij}\Delta\widecheck{g}_{ij}+\mathrm{Err}_{1}(\mathfrak{p})+{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathcal{H}}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),w_{2,\ell}\right\rangle,

\displaystyle F_{4,k}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)

\displaystyle=-{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{M}},\Omega^{-}_{ab}}(\vec{p})+\left\langle\breve{g}^{k\ell}\partial_{k}\widecheck{\pi}_{\ell i}-\mathrm{Err}_{2}(\mathfrak{p})_{i}+{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathcal{M}}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)_{i},(\Omega^{-}_{ab})^{i}\right\rangle,

\displaystyle F_{5,j,\ell}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)

\displaystyle=-\left\langle\breve{g}^{ij}\Delta\widecheck{g}_{ij}+\mathrm{Err}_{1}(\mathfrak{p})+{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathcal{H}}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),w_{j,\ell}\right\rangle,

\displaystyle F_{6,Z}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)

\displaystyle=\left\langle\breve{g}^{k\ell}\partial_{k}\widecheck{\pi}_{\ell i}-\mathrm{Err}_{2}(\mathfrak{p})_{i}+{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathcal{M}}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)_{i},Z^{i}\right\rangle,

and where it is implied that the last two equations of (
4.47
) must hold respectively for all
3\leq j\leq q
,
-(j-1)\leq\ell\leq j-1
and for all
Z\in\mathcal{Z}_{q}
. The following lemma allows us to invert these equations. We leave the proof to the reader since it directly follows from the construction of the families
(h_{j,\ell})_{3\leq j\leq q,-(j-1)\leq\ell\leq j-1}
and
(\varpi_{Z})_{Z\in\mathcal{Z}_{q}}
in Proposition
2.13
and in particular the invertibility of the matrices

\displaystyle\left(\left\langle h_{j,\ell},D{\mathcal{H}}[e,0]^{*}(w_{j^{\prime},\ell^{\prime}})\right\rangle\right)_{3\leq j,j^{\prime}\leq q,-(j-1)\leq\ell\leq j-1,-(j^{\prime}-1)\leq\ell^{\prime}\leq j^{\prime}-1}

and

\displaystyle\left(\left\langle\varpi_{Z},D{\mathcal{M}}[e,0]^{*}(Z^{\prime})\right\rangle\right)_{(Z,Z^{\prime})\in\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{2}}.

Lemma 4.10
.

Let
(G_{j,\ell})_{3\leq j\leq q,-(j-1)\leq\ell\leq j-1}
and
(P_{Z})_{Z\in\mathcal{Z}_{q}}
be two collections of real numbers. There exist two unique tensors
(\breve{g},\breve{\pi})\in\mathcal{A}_{q}\times\mathcal{B}_{q}
such that

\displaystyle\left\langle\breve{g},D{\mathcal{H}}[e,0]^{*}(w_{j,\ell})\right\rangle

\displaystyle=G_{j,\ell},

\displaystyle\left\langle\breve{\pi},D{\mathcal{M}}[e,0]^{*}(Z)\right\rangle

\displaystyle=P_{Z},

respectively for all
3\leq j\leq q
,
-(j-1)\leq\ell\leq j-1
and for all
Z\in\mathcal{Z}_{q}
. Moreover they satisfy

\displaystyle\left\|\breve{g}\right\|_{W^{2,\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\max_{3\leq j\leq q,-(j-1)\leq\ell\leq j-1}|G_{j,\ell}|,

\displaystyle\left\|\breve{\pi}\right\|_{W^{1,\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\max_{Z\in\mathcal{Z}_{q}}|P_{Z}|.

We now introduce the map which will allow us to solve system (
4.47
) via a fixed point argument.

Definition 4.11
.

For
D_{0}>0
, we define the following subset of the parameter space
P
:

\displaystyle B_{D_{0}}

\displaystyle=B_{{\mathbb{R}}}\left(0,D_{0}\eta^{2}\right)\times B_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\left(0,D_{0}\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{\eta}\right)^{2\theta}\right)\times B_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\left(0,D_{0}\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{\eta}\right)^{2\theta}\right)\times B_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\left(0,1-\frac{{\alpha}}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\times B_{\mathcal{A}_{q}}\left(0,D_{0}^{q+1}\eta\varepsilon\right)\times B_{\mathcal{B}_{q}}\left(0,D_{0}^{q+1}\eta\varepsilon\right).

The parameter map
\Psi^{(p)}
is defined by

\displaystyle\Psi^{(p)}:B_{D_{0}}\longrightarrow B_{D_{0}}

\displaystyle\hskip 41.25641pt\mathfrak{p}\longmapsto\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}

such that
\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}=\left(m^{\prime},y^{\prime},a^{\prime},v^{\prime},\breve{g}^{\prime},\breve{\pi}^{\prime}\right)
solves the system

\displaystyle 16\pi\gamma^{\prime}m^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\eta^{2}+F_{1}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),

(4.48)

\displaystyle 16\pi\gamma^{\prime}m^{\prime}(v^{\prime})^{k}

\displaystyle=-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\widecheck{\pi}^{ij}\partial_{k}\widecheck{g}_{ij}+F_{2,k}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),

(4.49)

\displaystyle m^{\prime}(y^{\prime})^{\ell^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}Q^{A}_{2,\ell}(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi})+\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}F_{3,\ell^{\prime}}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),

(4.50)

\displaystyle m^{\prime}(a^{\prime})^{k}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{8\pi}Q^{B}_{ab}(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi})+\frac{1}{8\pi}F_{4,k}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),

(4.51)

\displaystyle\left\langle\breve{g}^{\prime},D{\mathcal{H}}[e,0]^{*}(w_{j,\ell})\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-\left\langle{\mathcal{H}}\left(e+\chi_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}}(g_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}}\pi_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}}\right),w_{j,\ell}\right\rangle

(4.52)

\displaystyle\quad-Q^{A}_{j,\ell}(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi})+F_{5,j,\ell}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),

\displaystyle\left\langle\breve{\pi}^{\prime},D{\mathcal{M}}[e,0]^{*}(Z)\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-\left\langle{\mathcal{M}}\left(e+\chi_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}}(g_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}}\pi_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}}\right),Z\right\rangle

(4.53)

\displaystyle\quad+Q^{B}_{Z}(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi})+F_{6,Z}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right).

(4.54)

Note that the system (
4.48
)-(
4.53
) is not linear since the LHS of the equations (
4.48
)-(
4.51
) involve products of different components of
\vec{p}\,^{\prime}
and also since
\vec{p}\,^{\prime}
appears in a nonlinear way on the RHS of the equations (
4.52
)-(
4.53
) for
\breve{g}^{\prime}
and
\breve{\pi}^{\prime}
. It has nevertheless a triangular structure since
\vec{p}\,^{\prime}
will be defined through (
4.48
)-(
4.51
) and then
\breve{g}^{\prime}
and
\breve{\pi}^{\prime}
will be defined through (
4.52
) and (
4.53
).

Remark 4.12
.

If
\mathfrak{p}\in B_{D_{0}}
, then in particular
|v|\leq 1-\frac{{\alpha}}{2}
which implies

\displaystyle\gamma\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-\left(1-\frac{{\alpha}}{2}\right)^{2}}}.

Since
\varepsilon
will be chosen small compared to
{\alpha}
, and hence to
\gamma^{-1}
, in what follows, all the symbols
\lesssim_{\gamma}
(or
\lesssim_{\max(\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})}
if
\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}\in B_{D_{0}}
) will simply become
\lesssim
.

The next lemma gives estimates for the remainders of the equations (
4.48
)-(
4.53
).

Lemma 4.13
.

If
\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}\in B_{D_{0}}
then

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1,\dots,6}\left|F_{i}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{D_{0},C_{0}}\eta^{2}\varepsilon,

(4.55)

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1,2}\left|F_{i}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)-F_{i}\left(\mathfrak{p}^{\prime},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{D_{0},C_{0}}\eta d(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}),

(4.56)

\displaystyle\sum_{i=3,4}\left|F_{i}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)-F_{i}\left(\mathfrak{p}^{\prime},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{D_{0},C_{0}}\eta^{1-2\theta}\varepsilon^{2\theta}d(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}),

(4.57)

\displaystyle\sum_{i=5,6}\left|F_{i}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)-F_{i}\left(\mathfrak{p}^{\prime},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{D_{0},C_{0}}\varepsilon d(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}).

(4.58)

Proof.

We recall that
\widecheck{u}
and
\widecheck{X}
are assumed to satisfy (
4.46
). In order to prove (
4.55
), we note that all the remainders
F_{b}
for
b=1,\dots,6
are of the following schematic form
7
7

7

In this proof, the scalar product with
r^{j-1}
is an abuse of notations standing for a scalar product with a scalar function
f_{j}
satisfying
|f_{j}|+r|\nabla f_{j}|\lesssim r^{j-1}
.

\displaystyle F^{(i,{\mathcal{O}},Y,j)}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)={\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{O}},Y}(\vec{p})+\left\langle\breve{g}(\nabla^{2}\widecheck{g}+\nabla\widecheck{\pi})+\mathrm{Err}_{i}(\mathfrak{p})+{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathcal{O}}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),r^{j-1}\right\rangle,

(4.59)

for
i=1,2
,
{\mathcal{O}}={\mathcal{H}},{\mathcal{M}}
,
Y=1,x^{k},W_{1,0,k},\Omega^{-}_{ab}
and
1\leq j\leq q
. For the first term in (
4.59
) we simply use (
2.17
):

\displaystyle\left|{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{O}},Y}(\vec{p})\right|\lesssim_{D_{0}}\eta^{4}.

We now estimate each term in the scalar product in (
4.59
). For the first term, we use the support property of
\breve{g}
and integrate by parts:

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\breve{g}(\nabla^{2}\widecheck{g}+\nabla\widecheck{\pi}),r^{j-1}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\breve{g}\right\|_{W^{1,\infty}}\left(\left\|\nabla\widecheck{g}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\widecheck{\pi}\right\|_{L^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{D_{0}}\eta^{2}\varepsilon.

For the second term, we use (
4.14
) and
j\leq q
:

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\mathrm{Err}_{i}(\mathfrak{p}),r^{j-1}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon m\left\|(1+r)^{j-q-\delta-4}\right\|_{L^{1}}+\varepsilon\left(\left\|\nabla\widecheck{g}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\widecheck{\pi}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)+\left\|\breve{g}\right\|_{W^{2,\infty}}^{2}+\left\|\breve{\pi}\right\|_{W^{1,\infty}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\left\|\breve{g}\right\|_{W^{2,\infty}}+\left\|\breve{\pi}\right\|_{W^{1,\infty}}\right)\left(m+\left\|r^{-1}\widecheck{g}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\widecheck{g}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\widecheck{\pi}\right\|_{L^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{D_{0}}\eta^{2}\varepsilon.

For the third term, we use (
4.28
),
j\leq q
,
\delta>0
:

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathcal{O}}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),r^{j-1}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\left\|\nabla\widecheck{X}\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-1}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\widecheck{X}\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-1}}\left\|\nabla\widecheck{u}\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-1}}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\left(m\left\|(1+r)^{j-q-\delta-\frac{5}{2}}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|r^{-1}\widecheck{g}\right\|_{L^{2}}\right)\left(\left\|\nabla^{2}\widecheck{u}\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}}+\left\|\nabla^{2}\widecheck{X}\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\left(m\left\|(1+r)^{j-q-\delta-\frac{5}{2}}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\widecheck{g}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\widecheck{\pi}\right\|_{L^{2}}\right)\left(\left\|\nabla\widecheck{u}\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-1}}+\left\|\nabla\widecheck{X}\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-1}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\left|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}r^{j-1}\widecheck{u}\nabla^{2}\widecheck{g}\right|+m\left\|(1+r)^{j-q-\delta-\frac{5}{2}}\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|\widecheck{u}\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta}}+\left(\left\|\nabla\widecheck{g}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\widecheck{\pi}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)\left\|\widecheck{u}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\left(\left\|\breve{g}\right\|_{W^{2,\infty}}+\left\|\breve{\pi}\right\|_{W^{1,\infty}}\right)\left(\left\|\widecheck{u}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}+\left\|\widecheck{X}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim_{D_{0},C_{0}}\eta^{2}\varepsilon+\left|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}r^{j-1}\widecheck{u}\nabla^{2}\widecheck{g}\right|,

where for the terms multiplied by
\mathbbm{1}_{\{1\leq r\leq 10\}}
we used Cauchy-Schwarz inequality on a fixed bounded domain and added artificial weights as in
\left\|f\mathbbm{1}_{\{1\leq r\leq 10\}}\right\|_{L^{1}}\lesssim\left\|f\right\|_{L^{2}_{\mu}}
(which holds for any
\mu\in{\mathbb{R}}
). We integrate by parts the term
r^{j-1}\widecheck{u}\nabla^{2}\widecheck{g}
in order to get
\eta^{2}\varepsilon
instead of
\eta\varepsilon^{2}
:

\displaystyle\left|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}r^{j-1}\widecheck{u}\nabla^{2}\widecheck{g}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}r^{j-1}\nabla\widecheck{u}\nabla\widecheck{g}\right|+\left|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}r^{j-2}\widecheck{u}\nabla\widecheck{g}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\nabla\widecheck{g}\right\|_{L^{2}}\left(\left\|\nabla\widecheck{u}\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-1}}+\left\|\widecheck{u}\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{C_{0}}\eta^{2}\varepsilon.

We have proved that
\left|F^{(i,{\mathcal{O}},j)}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)\right|\lesssim_{D_{0},C_{0}}\eta^{2}\varepsilon
, which proves (
4.55
).

We now turn to the proof of (
4.56
)-(
4.58
). We have schematically

\displaystyle F^{(i,{\mathcal{O}},j)}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)-F^{(i,{\mathcal{O}},j)}\left(\mathfrak{p}^{\prime},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)

\displaystyle={\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{O}},Y}(\vec{p})-{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{O}},Y}(\vec{p}\,^{\prime})

(4.60)

\displaystyle\quad+\left\langle(\nabla^{2}\widecheck{g}+\nabla\widecheck{\pi})(\breve{g}-\breve{g}^{\prime}),r^{j-1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\left\langle\mathrm{Err}_{i}(\mathfrak{p})-\mathrm{Err}_{i}(\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}),r^{j-1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\left\langle{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathcal{O}}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)-{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathcal{O}}\left(\mathfrak{p}^{\prime},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),r^{j-1}\right\rangle.

We estimate each terms in (
4.60
). For the first term, we simply use (
2.18
):

\displaystyle\left|{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{O}},Y}(\vec{p})-{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{O}},Y}(\vec{p}\,^{\prime})\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{D_{0}}\eta d(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}).

For the second term in (
4.60
), we integrate by parts and use the support property of
\breve{g}-\breve{g}^{\prime}
:

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle(\nabla^{2}\widecheck{g}+\nabla\widecheck{\pi})(\breve{g}-\breve{g}^{\prime}),r^{j-1}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|\left\langle(\nabla\widecheck{g}+\widecheck{\pi})\nabla(\breve{g}-\breve{g}^{\prime}),r^{j-1}\right\rangle\right|+\left|\left\langle(\nabla\widecheck{g}+\widecheck{\pi})(\breve{g}-\breve{g}^{\prime}),r^{j-2}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\left\|\nabla\widecheck{g}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\widecheck{\pi}\right\|_{L^{2}}\right)\left\|\breve{g}-\breve{g}^{\prime}\right\|_{W^{2,\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\eta d(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}).

For the fourth term in (
4.60
), we use (
4.29
),
m+m^{\prime}\lesssim D_{0}\eta^{2}
and
j\leq q
:

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathcal{O}}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)-{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathcal{O}}\left(\mathfrak{p}^{\prime},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),r^{j-1}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\lesssim_{D_{0}}\left(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}r^{j-2}\left|\nabla^{2}\left(\widecheck{u}+\widecheck{X}\right)\right|+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}r^{j-3}\left|\nabla\left(\widecheck{u}+\widecheck{X}\right)\right|+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}r^{j-4}|\widecheck{u}|\right)d(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\quad+\left(\left\|\widecheck{u}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}+\left\|\widecheck{X}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}\right)d(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\lesssim_{D_{0}}\left(1+\left\|(1+r)^{j-q-\delta-\frac{5}{2}}\right\|_{L^{2}}\right)\left(\left\|\widecheck{u}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}+\left\|\widecheck{X}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}\right)d(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\lesssim_{D_{0},C_{0}}\eta\varepsilon d(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}).

For the third term in (
4.60
), we distinguish between
j=1
,
j=2
and
3\leq j\leq q
, which explains the difference between (
4.56
), (
4.57
) and (
4.58
). In all cases, we use (
4.16
),
m+m^{\prime}\lesssim D_{0}\eta^{2}
and

\displaystyle\left\|\breve{g}\right\|_{W^{2,\infty}}+\left\|\breve{g}^{\prime}\right\|_{W^{2,\infty}}+\left\|\breve{\pi}\right\|_{W^{1,\infty}}+\left\|\breve{\pi}^{\prime}\right\|_{W^{1,\infty}}\lesssim D_{0}^{q+1}\eta\varepsilon.

This gives

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\mathrm{Err}_{i}(\mathfrak{p})-\mathrm{Err}_{i}(\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}),r^{j-1}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|T_{1}^{abcd}\right|\left|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\partial_{a}\partial_{b}\widecheck{g}_{cd}(1+r)^{j-2}\right| +\left|S_{2}^{abc}\right|\left|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\partial_{a}\widecheck{\pi}_{bc}(1+r)^{j-2}\right|

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}(1+r)^{j-3}\left(|\nabla\widecheck{g}|+|\widecheck{\pi}|\right)+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}(1+r)^{j-4}|\widecheck{g}|\right)d(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}^{\prime})+C(D_{0})\eta d(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}(1+r)^{j-3}\left(|\nabla\widecheck{g}|+|\widecheck{\pi}|\right)+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}(1+r)^{j-4}|\widecheck{g}|\right)d(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}^{\prime})+C(D_{0})\eta d(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}),

where we integrated by parts in the first two integrals and used (
4.15
).

•

If
j=1
(corresponds to (
4.56
)), we can use the fact that
(1+r)^{-2}\in L^{2}
and obtain

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\mathrm{Err}_{i}(\mathfrak{p})-\mathrm{Err}_{i}(\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}),1\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|(1+r)^{-2}\right\|_{L^{2}}\left(\left\|\nabla\widecheck{g}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\widecheck{\pi}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|r^{-1}\widecheck{g}\right\|_{L^{2}}\right)d(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad+C(D_{0})\eta d(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\lesssim_{D_{0}}\eta d(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}).

•

If
j=2
(corresponds to (
4.57
)), we interpolate between the
L^{2}
norms and the weighted norms, thanks to Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and then a well-chosen Hölder’s inequality:

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}(1+r)^{-2}|\widecheck{g}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|(1+r)^{-3-\delta}\right\|_{L^{1}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}(1+r)^{-1+\delta}|\widecheck{g}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\left(\frac{|\widecheck{g}|^{2}}{r^{2}}\right)^{1-2\theta}\right\|_{L^{\frac{1}{1-2\theta}}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\|\left(r^{2q+2\delta-3}|\widecheck{g}|^{2}\right)^{2\theta}\right\|_{L^{\frac{1}{2\theta}}}^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\eta^{1-2\theta}\varepsilon^{2\theta},

and

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}(1+r)^{-1}\left(|\nabla\widecheck{g}|+|\widecheck{\pi}|\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|(1+r)^{-3-\delta}\right\|_{L^{1}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\left(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|\nabla\widecheck{g}|^{2}(1+r)^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\left(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|\widecheck{\pi}|^{2}(1+r)^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\eta^{1-2\theta}\varepsilon^{2\theta},

where we have used the computation (
4.38
) with
j=3
. This gives

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\mathrm{Err}_{i}(\mathfrak{p})-\mathrm{Err}_{i}(\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}),r\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{D_{0}}\eta^{1-2\theta}\varepsilon^{2\theta}d(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}).

•

If
3\leq j\leq q
(corresponds to (
4.58
)), we use the weighted norms of
\widecheck{g}
and
\widecheck{\pi}
:

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\mathrm{Err}_{i}(\mathfrak{p})-\mathrm{Err}_{i}(\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}),r^{j-1}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|(1+r)^{j-q-\delta-\frac{5}{2}}\right\|_{L^{2}}\left(\left\|\widecheck{g}\right\|_{H^{1}_{-q-\delta}}+\left\|\widecheck{\pi}\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-1}}\right)d(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad+C(D_{0})\eta d(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\lesssim_{D_{0}}\varepsilon d(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}).

This concludes the proof of Lemma
4.13
.
∎

4.4.2
A fixed point for the parameter map

Lemma 4.14
.

Consider
\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)\in H^{2}_{-q-\delta}\times H^{2}_{-q-\delta}
satisfying (
4.46
). If
D_{0}
is a large enough universal constant, and if
\varepsilon
is small enough compared to
C_{0}^{-1}
and
D_{0}^{-1}
, then the parameter map
\Psi^{(p)}
(see Definition
4.11
) is well-defined.

Proof.

Since the equations (
4.48
) and (
4.49
) are coupled through
\gamma^{\prime}
, the parameter map
\Psi^{(p)}
is a priori not well-defined. However the coupling is easy to untangle, since the quantity
16\pi\gamma^{\prime}m^{\prime}
(i.e. the LHS of (
4.48
)) appears on the LHS of (
4.49
). Hence, (
4.49
) rewrites

\displaystyle(v^{\prime})^{k}

\displaystyle=\frac{-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\widecheck{\pi}^{ij}\partial_{k}\widecheck{g}_{ij}+F_{2,k}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)}{\frac{1}{4}\left\|\nabla\widecheck{g}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\widecheck{\pi}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+F_{1}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)}

\displaystyle=\frac{-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\widecheck{\pi}^{ij}\partial_{k}\widecheck{g}_{ij}}{\frac{1}{4}\left\|\nabla\widecheck{g}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\widecheck{\pi}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}}\left(1+\frac{F_{1}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)}{\eta^{2}}\right)^{-1}+\frac{F_{2,k}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)}{\eta^{2}+F_{1}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)}

where we used the definition of
\eta
(see Theorem
3.8
). Thanks to (
4.55
) and of the bound

\displaystyle\left|\frac{-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\widecheck{\pi}^{ij}\partial_{k}\widecheck{g}_{ij}}{\frac{1}{4}\left\|\nabla\widecheck{g}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\widecheck{\pi}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}}\right|<1,

we obtain

\displaystyle(v^{\prime})^{k}

\displaystyle=\frac{-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\widecheck{\pi}^{ij}\partial_{k}\widecheck{g}_{ij}}{\frac{1}{4}\left\|\nabla\widecheck{g}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\widecheck{\pi}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}}+C(D_{0},C_{0})\varepsilon.

This defines the vector
v^{\prime}
and then implies
|v^{\prime}|=J(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi})+C(D_{0},C_{0})\varepsilon
(see Definition
3.6
for the definition of the functional
J(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi})
). Thanks to the assumption
(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi})\in V_{\alpha}
, we obtain

\displaystyle|v^{\prime}|\leq 1-{\alpha}+C(D_{0},C_{0})\varepsilon

and then
|v^{\prime}|\leq 1-\frac{{\alpha}}{2}
if
\varepsilon
is small compared to
C_{0}^{-1}
,
D_{0}^{-1}
and
{\alpha}
. This implies
1\leq\gamma^{\prime}\lesssim 1
according to Remark
4.12
.

We can now define
m^{\prime}
through (
4.48
) and with (
4.55
) obtain the bound

\displaystyle m^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\frac{\eta^{2}}{16\pi\gamma^{\prime}}\left(1+C(D_{0},C_{0})\varepsilon\right).

If
D_{0}
is large enough and if
\varepsilon
is small compared to
C_{0}^{-1}
and
D_{0}^{-1}
then we have the upper bound
m^{\prime}\leq D_{0}\eta^{2}
. Thanks to
\gamma^{\prime}\lesssim 1
, this also implies the lower bound
\eta^{2}\lesssim m^{\prime}
.

We can now define
y^{\prime}
and
a^{\prime}
through (
4.50
) and (
4.51
), after dividing by
m^{\prime}
using crucially the lower bound
\eta^{2}\lesssim m^{\prime}
. With (
4.55
) and
\left|Q^{A}_{2,\ell}(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi}) \right|+\left|Q^{B}_{ab}(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi}) \right|\lesssim\eta^{2(1-\theta)}\varepsilon^{2\theta}
(see Lemmas
4.7
and
4.8
) we obtain the bound

\displaystyle|y^{\prime}|+|a^{\prime}|\lesssim\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{\eta}\right)^{2\theta}+C(D_{0},C_{0})\varepsilon.

If
D_{0}
is large enough and
\varepsilon
is small compared to
C_{0}^{-1}
and
D_{0}^{-1}
(recall that
\eta\lesssim\varepsilon
) this implies the bound
|y^{\prime}|+|a^{\prime}|\leq D_{0}\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{\eta}\right)^{2\theta}
.

With the help of Lemma
4.10
we can uniquely define
\breve{g}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}_{q}
and
\breve{\pi}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{q}
as solutions of (
4.52
) and (
4.53
). Moreover, using
\left|Q^{A}_{j,\ell}(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi})\right|+\left|Q^{B}_{Z}(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi})\right|\lesssim\eta\varepsilon
from Lemmas
4.7
and
4.8
and (
4.55
), we obtain the bound

\displaystyle\left\|\breve{g}^{\prime}\right\|_{W^{2,\infty}}

\displaystyle+\left\|\breve{\pi}^{\prime}\right\|_{W^{1,\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\left(\left|{\mathcal{H}}\left(e+\chi_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}}(g_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}}\pi_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}}\right)\right|+\left|{\mathcal{M}}\left(e+\chi_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}}(g_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}}\pi_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}}\right)\right|\right)r^{q-1}

\displaystyle\quad+\eta\varepsilon\left(1+C(D_{0},C_{0})\eta\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim m^{\prime}\int_{\{\lambda^{\prime}\leq r\leq 2\lambda^{\prime}\}}r^{q-4}+\eta\varepsilon\left(1+C(D_{0},C_{0})\eta\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim m^{\prime}(\lambda^{\prime})^{q-1}+\eta\varepsilon\left(1+C(D_{0},C_{0})\eta\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim D_{0}^{q}\eta^{2-2(q-1)\theta}\varepsilon^{2(q-1)\theta}+\eta\varepsilon\left(1+C(D_{0},C_{0})\eta\right),

where we used (
2.23
) and the estimates obtained on
m^{\prime}
,
y^{\prime}
and
a^{\prime}
. Using now
2(q-1)\theta<1
we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\breve{g}^{\prime}\right\|_{W^{2,\infty}}+\left\|\breve{\pi}^{\prime}\right\|_{W^{1,\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\eta\varepsilon\left(1+D_{0}^{q}+C(D_{0},C_{0})\eta\right).

If
D_{0}
is large enough, and if
\varepsilon
is small compared to
C_{0}^{-1}
and
D_{0}^{-1}
we get the bound
\left\|\breve{g}^{\prime}\right\|_{W^{2,\infty}}+\left\|\breve{\pi}^{\prime}\right\|_{W^{1,\infty}}\leq D_{0}^{q+1}\eta\varepsilon
. We have proved that if
D_{0}
is large compared to universal constants, and
\varepsilon
is small compared to
D_{0}^{-1}
,
C_{0}^{-1}
and universal constants, the parameter map
\Psi^{(p)}
is well-defined. This concludes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Lemma 4.15
.

Consider
\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)\in H^{2}_{-q-\delta}\times H^{2}_{-q-\delta}
satisfying (
4.46
). If
D_{0}
is large enough and if
\varepsilon
is small enough compared to
D_{0}^{-1}
and
C_{0}^{-1}
, then the parameter map
\Psi^{(p)}
