is a contraction for the distance
d
defined in (
4.1
).

Proof.

Let
\mathfrak{p}_{1},\mathfrak{p}_{2}\in B_{D_{0}}
and let
\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}_{i}=\Psi^{(p)}(\mathfrak{p}_{i})
. By taking the difference of the equations defining
\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}_{i}
we obtain

\displaystyle 16\pi\left(\gamma^{\prime}_{1}m^{\prime}_{1}-\gamma^{\prime}_{2}m^{\prime}_{2}\right)

\displaystyle=F_{1}\left(\mathfrak{p}_{1},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)-F_{1}\left(\mathfrak{p}_{2},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),

(4.61)

\displaystyle 16\pi\left(\gamma^{\prime}_{1}m^{\prime}_{1}(v^{\prime}_{1})^{k}-\gamma^{\prime}_{2}m^{\prime}_{2}(v^{\prime}_{2})^{k}\right)

\displaystyle=F_{2,k}\left(\mathfrak{p}_{1},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)-F_{2,k}\left(\mathfrak{p}_{2},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),

(4.62)

\displaystyle\sqrt{3}\left(m^{\prime}_{1}(y^{\prime}_{1})^{\ell^{\prime}}-m^{\prime}_{2}(y^{\prime}_{2})^{\ell^{\prime}}\right)

\displaystyle=F_{3,\ell^{\prime}}\left(\mathfrak{p}_{1},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)-F_{3,\ell^{\prime}}\left(\mathfrak{p}_{2},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),

(4.63)

\displaystyle 8\pi\left(m^{\prime}_{1}(a^{\prime}_{1})^{k}-m^{\prime}_{2}(a^{\prime}_{2})^{k}\right)

\displaystyle=F_{4,k}\left(\mathfrak{p}_{1},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)-F_{4,k}\left(\mathfrak{p}_{2},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),

(4.64)

and

\displaystyle\left\langle\breve{g}^{\prime}_{1}-\breve{g}^{\prime}_{2},D{\mathcal{H}}[e,0]^{*}(w_{j,\ell})\right\rangle

(4.65)

\displaystyle\quad=\left\langle{\mathcal{H}}\left(e+\chi_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}_{2}}(g_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}_{2}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}_{2}}\pi_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}_{2}}\right)-{\mathcal{H}}\left(e+\chi_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}_{1}}(g_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}_{1}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}_{1}}\pi_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}_{1}}\right),w_{j,\ell}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\quad+F_{5,j,\ell}\left(\mathfrak{p}_{1},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)-F_{5,j,\ell}\left(\mathfrak{p}_{2},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),

\displaystyle\left\langle\breve{\pi}^{\prime}_{1}-\breve{\pi}^{\prime}_{2},D{\mathcal{M}}[e,0]^{*}(Z)\right\rangle

(4.66)

\displaystyle\quad=\left\langle{\mathcal{M}}\left(e+\chi_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}_{2}}(g_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}_{2}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}_{2}}\pi_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}_{2}}\right)-{\mathcal{M}}\left(e+\chi_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}_{1}}(g_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}_{1}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}_{1}}\pi_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}_{1}}\right),Z\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\quad F_{6,Z}\left(\mathfrak{p}_{1},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)-F_{6,Z}\left(\mathfrak{p}_{2},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right).

We start by deducing from (
4.62
) that

\displaystyle\eta^{2}\left|(v^{\prime}_{1})^{k}-(v^{\prime}_{2})^{k}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\eta^{2}}{\gamma^{\prime}_{1}m^{\prime}_{1}}\left|\gamma^{\prime}_{1}m^{\prime}_{1}-\gamma^{\prime}_{2}m^{\prime}_{2}\right|\left|(v^{\prime}_{2})^{k}\right|+\frac{\eta^{2}}{\gamma^{\prime}_{1}m^{\prime}_{1}}\left|F_{2,k}\left(\mathfrak{p}_{1},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)-F_{2,k}\left(\mathfrak{p}_{2},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{D_{0}}\left|F_{1}\left(\mathfrak{p}_{1},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)-F_{1}\left(\mathfrak{p}_{2},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)\right|+\left|F_{2,k}\left(\mathfrak{p}_{1},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)-F_{2,k}\left(\mathfrak{p}_{2},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)\right|

where we also used (
4.61
) and the estimates following from
\mathfrak{p}\in B_{D_{0}}
and the lower bound on the masses (recall also Remark
4.12
). Using (
4.56
) this gives

\displaystyle\eta^{2}\left|v^{\prime}_{1}-v^{\prime}_{2}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{D_{0},C_{0}}\eta d(\mathfrak{p}_{1},\mathfrak{p}_{2}).

(4.67)

We now deduce from (
4.61
) that

\displaystyle\left|m^{\prime}_{1}-m^{\prime}_{2}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{m^{\prime}_{2}}{\gamma^{\prime}_{1}}\left|\gamma^{\prime}_{1}-\gamma^{\prime}_{2}\right|+\left|F_{1}\left(\mathfrak{p}_{1},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)-F_{1}\left(\mathfrak{p}_{2},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\eta^{2}\left|v^{\prime}_{1}-v^{\prime}_{2}\right|+C(D_{0},C_{0})\eta d(\mathfrak{p}_{1},\mathfrak{p}_{2})

\displaystyle\lesssim_{D_{0},C_{0}}\eta d(\mathfrak{p}_{1},\mathfrak{p}_{2}),

(4.68)

where we used the fact that
x\mapsto\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-x^{2}}}
is Lipschitz on
\left[0,1-\frac{{\alpha}}{2}\right]
, (
4.67
) and (
4.56
). From (
4.63
) we get

\displaystyle\eta^{2}\left|(y^{\prime}_{1})^{\ell^{\prime}}-(y^{\prime}_{2})^{\ell^{\prime}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\eta^{2}|y^{\prime}_{2}|}{m^{\prime}_{1}}\left|m^{\prime}_{1}-m^{\prime}_{2}\right|+\frac{\eta^{2}}{m^{\prime}_{1}}\left|F_{3,\ell^{\prime}}\left(\mathfrak{p}_{1},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)-F_{3,\ell^{\prime}}\left(\mathfrak{p}_{2},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{D_{0}}\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{\eta}\right)^{2\theta}\left|m^{\prime}_{1}-m^{\prime}_{2}\right|+C(D_{0},C_{0})\eta^{1-2\theta}\varepsilon^{2\theta}d(\mathfrak{p}_{1},\mathfrak{p}_{2})

where we have used (
4.57
). Using (
4.68
) we obtain

\displaystyle\eta^{2}\left|y^{\prime}_{1}-y^{\prime}_{2}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{D_{0},C_{0}}\eta^{1-2\theta}\varepsilon^{2\theta}d(\mathfrak{p}_{1},\mathfrak{p}_{2}).

(4.69)

Estimating
\left|a^{\prime}_{1}-a^{\prime}_{2}\right|
from (
4.64
) is done identically, and we obtain

\displaystyle\eta^{2}\left|a^{\prime}_{1}-a^{\prime}_{2}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{D_{0},C_{0}}\eta^{1-2\theta}\varepsilon^{2\theta}d(\mathfrak{p}_{1},\mathfrak{p}_{2}).

(4.70)

Finally, we note that the invertibility of the matrices

\displaystyle\left(\left\langle h_{j,\ell},D{\mathcal{H}}[e,0]^{*}(w_{j^{\prime},\ell^{\prime}})\right\rangle\right)_{3\leq j,j^{\prime}\leq q,-(j-1)\leq\ell\leq j-1,-(j^{\prime}-1)\leq\ell^{\prime}\leq j^{\prime}-1}

and

\displaystyle\left(\left\langle\varpi_{Z},D{\mathcal{M}}[e,0]^{*}(Z^{\prime})\right\rangle\right)_{(Z,Z^{\prime})\in\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{2}}

allows us to deduce from (
4.65
) and (
4.66
) that

\displaystyle\left\|\breve{g}^{\prime}_{1}-\breve{g}^{\prime}_{2}\right\|_{W^{2,\infty}}

\displaystyle+\left\|\breve{\pi}^{\prime}_{1}-\breve{\pi}^{\prime}_{2}\right\|_{W^{1,\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\left|{\mathcal{H}}\left(e+\chi_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}_{2}}(g_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}_{2}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}_{2}}\pi_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}_{2}}\right)-{\mathcal{H}}\left(e+\chi_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}_{1}}(g_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}_{1}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}_{1}}\pi_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}_{1}}\right)\right|r^{q-1}

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\left|{\mathcal{M}}\left(e+\chi_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}_{2}}(g_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}_{2}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}_{2}}\pi_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}_{2}}\right)-{\mathcal{M}}\left(e+\chi_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}_{1}}(g_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}_{1}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}_{1}}\pi_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}_{1}}\right)\right|r^{q-1}

\displaystyle\quad+C(D_{0},C_{0})\varepsilon d(\mathfrak{p}_{1},\mathfrak{p}_{2}),

where we have used (
4.58
). Using now (
2.24
) we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\breve{g}^{\prime}_{1}-\breve{g}^{\prime}_{2}\right\|_{W^{2,\infty}}+\left\|\breve{\pi}^{\prime}_{1}-\breve{\pi}^{\prime}_{2}\right\|_{W^{1,\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(|m^{\prime}_{1}-m^{\prime}_{2}|+\eta^{2}|v^{\prime}_{1}-v^{\prime}_{2}|\right)\int_{\{\min(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})\leq r\leq 2\max(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})\}}r^{q-4}

\displaystyle\quad+\eta^{2}\left(|y^{\prime}_{1}-y^{\prime}_{2}|+|a^{\prime}_{1}-a^{\prime}_{2}|\right)\int_{\{\min(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})\leq r\leq 2\max(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})\}}r^{q-5}

\displaystyle\quad+C(D_{0},C_{0})\varepsilon d(\mathfrak{p}_{1},\mathfrak{p}_{2})

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(|m^{\prime}_{1}-m^{\prime}_{2}|+\eta^{2}|v^{\prime}_{1}-v^{\prime}_{2}|\right)\max(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})^{q-1}

\displaystyle\quad+\eta^{2}\left(|y^{\prime}_{1}-y^{\prime}_{2}|+|a^{\prime}_{1}-a^{\prime}_{2}|\right)\max(\lambda_{1},\lambda^{\prime}_{2})^{q-2}

\displaystyle\quad+C(D_{0},C_{0})\varepsilon d(\mathfrak{p}_{1},\mathfrak{p}_{2}).

Using now (
4.67
), (
4.68
), (
4.69
), (
4.70
) and
2(q-1)\theta<1
we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\breve{g}^{\prime}_{1}-\breve{g}^{\prime}_{2}\right\|_{W^{2,\infty}}+\left\|\breve{\pi}^{\prime}_{1}-\breve{\pi}^{\prime}_{2}\right\|_{W^{1,\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{D_{0},C_{0}}\eta^{1-2(q-1)\theta}\varepsilon^{2(q-1)\theta}d(\mathfrak{p}_{1},\mathfrak{p}_{2})+\varepsilon d(\mathfrak{p}_{1},\mathfrak{p}_{2})

\displaystyle\lesssim_{D_{0},C_{0}}\varepsilon d(\mathfrak{p}_{1},\mathfrak{p}_{2}).

Putting everything together, we obtain

\displaystyle d\left(\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}_{1},\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{D_{0},C_{0}}\varepsilon d(\mathfrak{p}_{1},\mathfrak{p}_{2}).

Letting
\varepsilon
be small compared to
D_{0}^{-1}
and
C_{0}^{-1}
, we have proved that the parameter map
\Psi^{(p)}
is a contraction.
∎

The Banach fixed point theorem and the fact that the system (
4.47
) is equivalent to the orthogonality conditions (
4.7
)-(
4.10
) then imply the following statement on the parameter
\mathfrak{p}\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)
.

Corollary 4.16
.

Consider
\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)\in H^{2}_{-q-\delta}\times H^{2}_{-q-\delta}
satisfying (
4.46
). If
\varepsilon>0
is small enough compared to
C_{0}^{-1}
, there exists a unique
\mathfrak{p}\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)\in P
such that the orthogonality conditions (
4.7
)-(
4.10
) hold and such that the following bounds hold

\displaystyle\eta^{2}\lesssim m\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)\lesssim\eta^{2},\qquad\left|y\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)\right|+\left|a\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)\right|\lesssim\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{\eta}\right)^{2\theta},\qquad\left|v\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)\right|\leq 1-\frac{{\alpha}}{2},

and

\displaystyle\left\|\breve{g}\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)\right\|_{W^{2,\infty}}+\left\|\breve{\pi}\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)\right\|_{W^{1,\infty}}\lesssim\eta\varepsilon.

Remark 4.17
.

Following Remarks
4.9
, we note that in the case
q=1
the proof of Corollary
4.16
considerably simplifies mainly because we don’t need to solve equations (
4.50
) and (
4.51
), and hence we don’t need to divide by the mass.

4.4.3
A priori estimates on
\mathfrak{p}\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)

In order to solve for
\widecheck{u}
and
\widecheck{X}
, we will need to estimate how
\mathfrak{p}\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)
obtained in Corollary
4.16
depends on
\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)
. This is provided by the next lemma.

Lemma 4.18
.

Consider
\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),\left(\widecheck{u^{\prime}},\widecheck{X^{\prime}}\right)\in H^{2}_{-q-\delta}\times H^{2}_{-q-\delta}
both satisfying (
4.46
). If
\varepsilon>0
is small enough compared to
C_{0}^{-1}
, we have

\displaystyle\left|m\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)-m\left(\widecheck{u}^{\prime},\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right)\right|

\displaystyle+\eta^{2}\left|v\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)-v\left(\widecheck{u}^{\prime},\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{C_{0}}\eta\left(\left\|\widecheck{u}-\widecheck{u}^{\prime}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}+\left\|\widecheck{X}-\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}\right),

(4.71)

\displaystyle\eta^{2}\Big(\left|y\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)-y\left(\widecheck{u}^{\prime},\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right)\right|

\displaystyle+\left|a\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)-a\left(\widecheck{u}^{\prime},\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right)\right|\Big)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{C_{0}}\eta^{1-2\theta}\varepsilon^{2\theta}\left(\left\|\widecheck{u}-\widecheck{u}^{\prime}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}+\left\|\widecheck{X}-\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}\right),

(4.72)

\displaystyle\left\|\breve{g}\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)-\breve{g}\left(\widecheck{u}^{\prime},\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right)\right\|_{W^{2,\infty}}

\displaystyle+\left\|\breve{\pi}\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)-\breve{\pi}\left(\widecheck{u}^{\prime},\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right)\right\|_{W^{1,\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{C_{0}}\varepsilon\left(\left\|\widecheck{u}-\widecheck{u}^{\prime}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}+\left\|\widecheck{X}-\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}\right).

(4.73)

Proof.

For simplicity, we use the notation
\mathfrak{p}=\mathfrak{p}\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)
and
\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}=\mathfrak{p}\left(\widecheck{u}^{\prime},\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right)
. We substract the two systems satisfied by
\mathfrak{p}
and
\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}
and obtain

\displaystyle 16\pi\left(\gamma m-\gamma^{\prime}m^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle=F_{1}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)-F_{1}\left(\mathfrak{p}^{\prime},\widecheck{u}^{\prime},\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right),

(4.74)

\displaystyle 16\pi\left(\gamma mv^{k}-\gamma^{\prime}m^{\prime}(v^{\prime})^{k}\right)

\displaystyle=F_{2,k}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)-F_{2,k}\left(\mathfrak{p}^{\prime},\widecheck{u}^{\prime},\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right),

(4.75)

\displaystyle\sqrt{3}\left(my^{\ell^{\prime}}-m^{\prime}(y^{\prime})^{\ell^{\prime}}\right)

\displaystyle=F_{3,\ell^{\prime}}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)-F_{3,\ell^{\prime}}\left(\mathfrak{p}^{\prime},\widecheck{u}^{\prime},\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right),

(4.76)

\displaystyle 8\pi\left(ma^{k}-m^{\prime}(a^{\prime})^{k}\right)

\displaystyle=F_{4,k}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)-F_{4,k}\left(\mathfrak{p}^{\prime},\widecheck{u}^{\prime},\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right),

(4.77)

and

\displaystyle\left\langle\breve{g}-\breve{g}^{\prime},D{\mathcal{H}}[e,0]^{*}(w_{j,\ell})\right\rangle

(4.78)

\displaystyle\quad=\left\langle{\mathcal{H}}\left(e+\chi_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}}(g_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}}\pi_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}}\right)-{\mathcal{H}}\left(e+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}\right),w_{j,\ell}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\quad+F_{5,j,\ell}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)-F_{5,j,\ell}\left(\mathfrak{p}^{\prime},\widecheck{u}^{\prime},\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right),

\displaystyle\left\langle\breve{\pi}-\breve{\pi}^{\prime},D{\mathcal{M}}[e,0]^{*}(Z)\right\rangle

(4.79)

\displaystyle\quad=\left\langle{\mathcal{M}}\left(e+\chi_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}}(g_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}}\pi_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}}\right)-{\mathcal{M}}\left(e+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}\right),Z\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\quad+F_{6,Z}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)-F_{6,Z}\left(\mathfrak{p}^{\prime},\widecheck{u}^{\prime},\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right).

As in the proof of Lemma
4.15
, we deduce from the system (
4.74
)-(
4.79
) that

\displaystyle|m-m^{\prime}|+\eta^{2}|v-v^{\prime}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{i=1,2}\left|F_{i}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)-F_{i}\left(\mathfrak{p}^{\prime},\widecheck{u}^{\prime},\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right)\right|,

(4.80)

\displaystyle\eta^{2}\left(|y-y^{\prime}|+|a-a^{\prime}|\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{\eta}\right)^{2\theta}\sum_{i=1,2}\left|F_{i}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)-F_{i}\left(\mathfrak{p}^{\prime},\widecheck{u}^{\prime},\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right)\right|

(4.81)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{i=3,4}\left|F_{i}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)-F_{i}\left(\mathfrak{p}^{\prime},\widecheck{u}^{\prime},\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right)\right|,

\displaystyle\left\|\breve{g}-\breve{g}^{\prime}\right\|_{W^{2,\infty}}+\left\|\breve{\pi}-\breve{\pi}^{\prime}\right\|_{W^{1,\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{\eta}\right)^{2(q-1)\theta}\sum_{i=1,2}\left|F_{i}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)-F_{i}\left(\mathfrak{p}^{\prime},\widecheck{u}^{\prime},\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right)\right|

(4.82)

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{\eta}\right)^{2(q-2)\theta}\sum_{i=3,4}\left|F_{i}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)-F_{i}\left(\mathfrak{p}^{\prime},\widecheck{u}^{\prime},\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{i=5,6}\left|F_{i}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)-F_{i}\left(\mathfrak{p}^{\prime},\widecheck{u}^{\prime},\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right)\right|.

The triangle inequality implies for each
i=1,\dots,6
that

\displaystyle\left|F_{i}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)-F_{i}\left(\mathfrak{p}^{\prime},\widecheck{u}^{\prime},\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right)\right|

(4.83)

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\left|F_{i}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)-F_{i}\left(\mathfrak{p}^{\prime},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)\right|+\left|F_{i}\left(\mathfrak{p}^{\prime},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)-F_{i}\left(\mathfrak{p}^{\prime},\widecheck{u}^{\prime},\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right)\right|.

The first term in (
4.83
) can be estimated by (
4.56
), (
4.57
) and (
4.58
), depending on the value of
i
. We now estimate the second term. Thanks to the expression of the terms
F_{i}
, this amounts to estimating a term of the form
8
8

8

In this proof, the scalar product with
r^{j-1}
is an abuse of notations standing for a scalar product with a scalar function
f_{j}
satisfying
|f_{j}|+r|\nabla f_{j}|\lesssim r^{j-1}
.

\displaystyle\left\langle{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathcal{O}}\left(\mathfrak{p}^{\prime},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)-{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathcal{O}}\left(\mathfrak{p}^{\prime},\widecheck{u}^{\prime},\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right),r^{j-1}\right\rangle

for
1\leq j\leq q
and
{\mathcal{O}}
denoting either
{\mathcal{H}}
or
{\mathcal{M}}
. We use (
4.29
)

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathcal{O}}\left(\mathfrak{p}^{\prime},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)-{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathcal{O}}\left(\mathfrak{p}^{\prime},\widecheck{u}^{\prime},\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right),r^{j-1}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\left|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}r^{j-1}(\widecheck{u}-\widecheck{u}^{\prime})\nabla^{2}\widecheck{g}\right|+\eta^{2}\left\|(1+r)^{j-q-\delta-\frac{5}{2}}\right\|_{L^{2}}\left(\left\|\widecheck{u}-\widecheck{u}^{\prime}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}+\left\|\widecheck{X}-\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\left(\left\|\nabla\widecheck{u}\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-1}}+\left\|\nabla\widecheck{X}\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-1}}+\left\|\nabla\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-1}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt\times\left(\left\|\nabla(\widecheck{u}-\widecheck{u}^{\prime})\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-1}}+\left\|\nabla\left(\widecheck{X}-\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-1}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\left\|r^{-1}\widecheck{g}\right\|_{L^{2}}\left(\left\|\nabla^{2}(\widecheck{u}-\widecheck{u}^{\prime})\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}}+\left\|\nabla^{2}\left(\widecheck{X}-\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\left(\left\|\nabla\widecheck{g}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\widecheck{\pi}\right\|_{L^{2}}\right)\left(\left\|\nabla(\widecheck{u}-\widecheck{u}^{\prime})\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-1}}+\left\|\nabla\left(\widecheck{X}-\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-1}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\left(\left\|\nabla\widecheck{g}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\widecheck{\pi}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)\left\|\widecheck{u}-\widecheck{u}^{\prime}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}+\eta\varepsilon\left(\left\|\widecheck{u}-\widecheck{u}^{\prime}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}+\left\|\widecheck{X}-\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}\right).

We integrate by parts in the integral involving
\nabla^{2}\widecheck{g}
and obtain

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathcal{O}}\left(\mathfrak{p}^{\prime},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)-{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathcal{O}}\left(\mathfrak{p}^{\prime},\widecheck{u}^{\prime},\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right),r^{j-1}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim_{C_{0}}\eta\left(\left\|\widecheck{u}-\widecheck{u}^{\prime}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}+\left\|\widecheck{X}-\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|\nabla r^{j-1}||\widecheck{u}-\widecheck{u}^{\prime}||\nabla\widecheck{g}|+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}r^{j-1}|\nabla(\widecheck{u}-\widecheck{u}^{\prime})||\nabla\widecheck{g}|

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim_{C_{0}}\eta\left(\left\|\widecheck{u}-\widecheck{u}^{\prime}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}+\left\|\widecheck{X}-\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}\right).

Together with (
4.83
), (
4.56
), (
4.57
) and (
4.58
), this implies

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1,2}\left|F_{i}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)-F_{i}\left(\mathfrak{p}^{\prime},\widecheck{u}^{\prime},\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right)\right|\lesssim_{C_{0}}\eta d(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}^{\prime})+\eta\left(\left\|\widecheck{u}-\widecheck{u}^{\prime}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}+\left\|\widecheck{X}-\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}\right),

\displaystyle\sum_{i=3,4}

\displaystyle\left|F_{i}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)-F_{i}\left(\mathfrak{p}^{\prime},\widecheck{u}^{\prime},\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim_{C_{0}}\eta^{1-2\theta}\varepsilon^{2\theta}d(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}^{\prime})+\eta\left(\left\|\widecheck{u}-\widecheck{u}^{\prime}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}+\left\|\widecheck{X}-\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}\right),

\displaystyle\sum_{i=5,6}\left|F_{i}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)-F_{i}\left(\mathfrak{p}^{\prime},\widecheck{u}^{\prime},\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right)\right|\lesssim_{C_{0}}\varepsilon d(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}^{\prime})+\eta\left(\left\|\widecheck{u}-\widecheck{u}^{\prime}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}+\left\|\widecheck{X}-\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}\right).

Plugging this in (
4.80
), (
4.81
) and (
4.82
) we obtain

\displaystyle|m-m^{\prime}|+\eta^{2}|v-v^{\prime}|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{C_{0}}\eta\left(d(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}^{\prime})+\left\|\widecheck{u}-\widecheck{u}^{\prime}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}+\left\|\widecheck{X}-\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}\right),

(4.84)

\displaystyle\eta^{2}\left(|y-y^{\prime}|+|a-a^{\prime}|\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{C_{0}}\eta^{1-2\theta}\varepsilon^{2\theta}\left(d(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}^{\prime})+\left\|\widecheck{u}-\widecheck{u}^{\prime}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}+\left\|\widecheck{X}-\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}\right),

(4.85)

\displaystyle\left\|\breve{g}-\breve{g}^{\prime}\right\|_{W^{2,\infty}}+\left\|\breve{\pi}-\breve{\pi}^{\prime}\right\|_{W^{1,\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{C_{0}}\varepsilon\left(d(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}^{\prime})+\left\|\widecheck{u}-\widecheck{u}^{\prime}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}+\left\|\widecheck{X}-\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}\right).

(4.86)

We sum these inequalities and after taking
\varepsilon
small enough compared to
C_{0}^{-1}
we obtain

\displaystyle d(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}^{\prime})\lesssim_{C_{0}}\varepsilon\left(\left\|\widecheck{u}-\widecheck{u}^{\prime}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}+\left\|\widecheck{X}-\widecheck{X}^{\prime}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}\right).

We plug this into (
4.84
), (
4.85
) and (
4.86
) and finally obtain (
4.71
), (
4.72
) and (
4.73
).
∎

4.5
Solving for
\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)

In this section, we solve (
4.4
) with a fixed point argument. In order to get the appropriate decay for the solutions
u
and
X
, we set the parameter
\mathfrak{p}
appearing in (
4.4
) to be the one constructed in Section
4.4
. Therefore, the system we wish to solve is

\left\{\begin{aligned} 8\Delta\widecheck{u}&=A\left(\mathfrak{p}\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),\\
\Delta\widecheck{X}_{i}&=B\left(\mathfrak{p}\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)_{i}.\end{aligned}\right.

(4.87)

Definition 4.19
.

Let
C_{0}>0
. The solution map
\Psi^{(s)}
is defined by

\displaystyle\Psi^{(s)}:B_{\left(H^{2}_{-q-\delta}\right)^{4}}(0,C_{0}\eta\varepsilon)\longrightarrow B_{\left(H^{2}_{-q-\delta}\right)^{4}}(0,C_{0}\eta\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\hskip 76.82234pt\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)\longmapsto\left(\Psi^{(s)}_{1}\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),\Psi^{(s)}_{2}\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)\right)

such that
\left(\Psi^{(s)}_{1}\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),\Psi^{(s)}_{2}\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)\right)
solves the system

\displaystyle 8\Delta\Psi^{(s)}_{1}\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)

\displaystyle=A\left(\mathfrak{p}\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),

(4.88)

\displaystyle\Delta\Psi^{(s)}_{2}\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)_{i}

\displaystyle=B\left(\mathfrak{p}\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)_{i},

(4.89)

where
\mathfrak{p}\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)
is defined in Corollary
4.16
.

Lemma 4.20
.

If
C_{0}
is large enough and
\varepsilon
is small compared to
C_{0}^{-1}
, the solution map
\Psi^{(s)}
is well-defined.

Proof.

In order to show that the solution map
\Psi^{(s)}
is well-defined, we fix

\displaystyle\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)\in B_{\left(H^{2}_{-q-\delta}\right)^{4}}(0,C_{0}\eta\varepsilon)

and solve (
4.88
)-(
4.89
). First, note that if
C_{0}
is large enough and
\varepsilon
is small compared to
C_{0}^{-1}
, Corollary
4.16
applies and the parameter
\mathfrak{p}\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)
exists and satisfies all the bounds of Corollary
4.16
. For the sake of clarity, we simply denote
\mathfrak{p}\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)
by
\mathfrak{p}
,
m\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)
by
m
and so on.

We want to apply Proposition
2.7
to the system (
4.88
)-(
4.89
). In order to do that, we only need to estimate the
L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}
norm of
A\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)
and
B\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)
. We have

\displaystyle\left\|A\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}}+\left\|B\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}}

(4.90)

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\left\|{\mathcal{H}}(\bar{g}(\mathfrak{p}),\bar{\pi}(\mathfrak{p}))\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}}+\left\|{\mathcal{M}}(\bar{g}(\mathfrak{p}),\bar{\pi}(\mathfrak{p}))\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\left\|{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathcal{H}}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}}+\left\|{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathcal{M}}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}}.

We start with the first two norms in (
4.90
), using the expansions of Proposition
4.4
. From (
4.12
) we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|{\mathcal{H}}(\bar{g}(\mathfrak{p}),\bar{\pi}(\mathfrak{p}))\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}}

(4.91)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|{\mathcal{H}}\left(e+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}}+\left\|D{\mathcal{H}}[e,0]\left(\breve{g}\right)+\breve{g}^{ij}\Delta\widecheck{g}_{ij}\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}}

\displaystyle\quad+\left\|D^{2}{\mathcal{H}}[e,0]\left(\left(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi}\right),\left(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi}\right)\right)\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}}+\left\|\mathrm{Err}_{1}(\mathfrak{p})\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}}.

We estimate each terms in (
4.91
).

•

For the first term, we use (
2.23
)

\displaystyle\left\|{\mathcal{H}}\left(e+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim m\left\|r^{q+\delta-\frac{5}{2}}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\{\lambda\leq r\leq 2\lambda\}\right)}

\displaystyle\lesssim m\lambda^{q+\delta-1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\eta^{2-2(q+\delta-1)\theta}\varepsilon^{2(q+\delta-1)\theta}

\displaystyle\lesssim\eta\varepsilon,

where we used the estimates of Corollary
4.16
and
2(q+\delta-1)\theta<1
.

•

For the second term, we use the support property of
\breve{g}
and (
2.10
) and obtain

\displaystyle\left\|D{\mathcal{H}}[e,0]\left(\breve{g}\right)+\breve{g}^{ij}\Delta\widecheck{g}_{ij}\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\breve{g}\right\|_{W^{2,\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\eta\varepsilon.

•

For the third term, we have from (
2.11
)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{2}{\mathcal{H}}[e,0]\left(\left(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi}\right),\left(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi}\right)\right)\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|(1+r)^{q+\delta+\frac{1}{2}}\widecheck{g}\nabla^{2}\widecheck{g}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|(1+r)^{q+\delta+\frac{1}{2}}(\nabla\widecheck{g})^{2}\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+\left\|(1+r)^{q+\delta+\frac{1}{2}}(\widecheck{\pi})^{2}\right\|_{L^{2}}.

We use Hardy’s inequality and the embedding
H^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}\subset C^{0}_{-q-\delta-2}
from Lemma
2.3
to get

\displaystyle\left\|(1+r)^{q+\delta+\frac{1}{2}}\widecheck{g}\nabla^{2}\widecheck{g}\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|r^{-1}\widecheck{g}\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|(1+r)^{q+\delta+\frac{3}{2}}\nabla^{2}\widecheck{g}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\nabla\widecheck{g}\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|\nabla^{2}\widecheck{g}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\eta\varepsilon.

We use again the embedding
H^{2}_{-q-\delta-1}\subset C^{0}_{-q-\delta-1}
from Lemma
2.3
to get

\displaystyle\left\|(1+r)^{q+\delta+\frac{1}{2}}(\nabla\widecheck{g})^{2}\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\left\|(1+r)^{q+\delta+\frac{1}{2}}(\widecheck{\pi})^{2}\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\nabla\widecheck{g}\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|(1+r)^{q+\delta+\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\widecheck{g}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}+\left\|\widecheck{\pi}\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|(1+r)^{q+\delta+\frac{1}{2}}\widecheck{\pi}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\nabla\widecheck{g}\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|\nabla\widecheck{g}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta-1}}+\left\|\widecheck{\pi}\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|\widecheck{\pi}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta-1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\eta\varepsilon.

We have proved that

\displaystyle\left\|D^{2}{\mathcal{H}}[e,0]\left(\left(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi}\right),\left(\widecheck{g},\widecheck{\pi}\right)\right)\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\eta\varepsilon.

•

For the fourth term, we use (
4.14
) and the boundedness of
\widecheck{g}
,
\nabla\widecheck{g}
and
\widecheck{\pi}
to obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\mathrm{Err}_{1}(\mathfrak{p})\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon m\left\|(1+r)^{-\frac{5}{2}}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\varepsilon\left\|(1+r)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(|\nabla\widecheck{g}|^{2}+|\widecheck{\pi}|^{2}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+\left\|\breve{g}\right\|_{W^{2,\infty}}+\left\|\breve{\pi}\right\|_{W^{1,\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\eta\varepsilon.

We have proved that
\left\|{\mathcal{H}}(\bar{g}(\mathfrak{p}),\bar{\pi}(\mathfrak{p}))\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}}\lesssim\eta\varepsilon
. Estimating
\left\|{\mathcal{M}}(\bar{g}(\mathfrak{p}),\bar{\pi}(\mathfrak{p}))\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}}
is done in an identical way using (
4.13
) instead of (
4.12
). We thus have

\displaystyle\left\|{\mathcal{H}}(\bar{g}(\mathfrak{p}),\bar{\pi}(\mathfrak{p}))\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}}+\left\|{\mathcal{M}}(\bar{g}(\mathfrak{p}),\bar{\pi}(\mathfrak{p}))\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\eta\varepsilon.

(4.92)

We now estimate the last two norms in (
4.90
) using (
4.28
), the embedding
H^{1}_{-q-\delta-1}\times H^{1}_{-q-\delta-1}\subset L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}
from Lemma
2.3
and rough estimates like
(1+r)\left(|\nabla\widecheck{g}|+|\widecheck{\pi}|\right)\lesssim\varepsilon
:

\displaystyle\left\|{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathcal{H}}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}}+\left\|{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathcal{M}}\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\left\|\widecheck{u}\nabla^{2}\widecheck{g}\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}}+\left\|\left(\nabla\widecheck{X}\right)^{2}\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}}+\left\|\nabla\widecheck{u}\nabla\widecheck{X}\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\left\|\left(\frac{m}{1+r}+|\widecheck{g}|\right)\nabla^{2}\left(\widecheck{u}+\widecheck{X}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\left\|\left(\frac{m}{(1+r)^{2}}+|\nabla\widecheck{g}|+|\widecheck{\pi}|\right)\nabla\left(\widecheck{X}+\widecheck{u}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\left\|\left(\frac{m}{(1+r)^{3}}+\left(\frac{m}{(1+r)^{2}}+|\nabla\widecheck{g}|+|\widecheck{\pi}|\right)^{2}\right)\widecheck{u}\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+C\left(\left\|\breve{g}\right\|_{W^{2,\infty}},\left\|\breve{\pi}\right\|_{W^{1,\infty}}\right)\left(\left\|\widecheck{u}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}+\left\|\widecheck{X}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\varepsilon\left\|\widecheck{u}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}+\left\|\nabla\widecheck{X}\right\|_{H^{1}_{-q-\delta-1}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\widecheck{X}\right\|_{H^{1}_{-q-\delta-1}}\left\|\nabla\widecheck{u}\right\|_{H^{1}_{-q-\delta-1}}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\left(\eta^{2}+\varepsilon+\eta\varepsilon\right)\left(\left\|\widecheck{u}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}+\left\|\widecheck{X}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim C(C_{0})\eta\varepsilon^{2}.

Together with (
4.90
) and (
4.92
) this implies

\displaystyle\left\|A\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}}+\left\|B\left(\mathfrak{p},\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\eta\varepsilon\left(1+C(C_{0})\varepsilon\right).

(4.93)

Since the parameter
\mathfrak{p}
has been constructed such that the orthogonality conditions (
4.7
)-(
4.10
) hold (recall Corollary
4.16
), Proposition
2.7
implies the existence of a unique couple

\displaystyle\left(\Psi^{(s)}_{1}\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),\Psi^{(s)}_{2}\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)\right)\in H^{2}_{-q-\delta}

solving (
4.88
)-(
4.89
). Moreover, thanks to (
4.93
) we have

\displaystyle\left\|\Psi^{(s)}_{1}\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}+\left\|\Psi^{(s)}_{2}\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}\lesssim\eta\varepsilon\left(1+C(C_{0})\varepsilon\right).

Therefore, if
C_{0}
is large enough and if
\varepsilon
is small compared to
C_{0}^{-1}
, we have

\displaystyle\left(\Psi^{(s)}_{1}\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right),\Psi^{(s)}_{2}\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)\right)\in B_{\left(H^{2}_{-q-\delta}\right)^{4}}(0,C_{0}\eta\varepsilon).

This shows that the solution map
\Psi^{(s)}
is well-defined.
∎

Lemma 4.21
.

If
C_{0}
is large enough and
\varepsilon
is small compared to
C_{0}^{-1}
, the solution map
\Psi^{(s)}
is a contraction in
H^{2}_{-q-\delta}
.

Proof.

Let
\left(\widecheck{u}_{a},\widecheck{X}_{a}\right)
and
\left(\widecheck{u}_{b},\widecheck{X}_{b}\right)
both in
B_{\left(H^{2}_{-q-\delta}\right)^{4}}(0,C_{0}\eta\varepsilon)
. We wish to estimate the difference

\displaystyle\Psi^{(s)}_{i}\left(\widecheck{u}_{a},\widecheck{X}_{a}\right)-\Psi^{(s)}_{i}\left(\widecheck{u}_{b},\widecheck{X}_{b}\right)

for
i=1,2
. For this, we write the equations satisfied by these differences:

\displaystyle 8\Delta\left(\Psi^{(s)}_{1}\left(\widecheck{u}_{a},\widecheck{X}_{a}\right)-\Psi^{(s)}_{1}\left(\widecheck{u}_{b},\widecheck{X}_{b}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=A\left(\mathfrak{p}\left(\widecheck{u}_{a},\widecheck{X}_{a}\right),\widecheck{u}_{a},\widecheck{X}_{a}\right)-A\left(\mathfrak{p}\left(\widecheck{u}_{b},\widecheck{X}_{b}\right),\widecheck{u}_{b},\widecheck{X}_{b}\right),

(4.94)

\displaystyle\Delta\left(\Psi^{(s)}_{2}\left(\widecheck{u}_{a},\widecheck{X}_{a}\right)-\Psi^{(s)}_{2}\left(\widecheck{u}_{b},\widecheck{X}_{b}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=B\left(\mathfrak{p}\left(\widecheck{u}_{a},\widecheck{X}_{a}\right),\widecheck{u}_{a},\widecheck{X}_{a}\right)-B\left(\mathfrak{p}\left(\widecheck{u}_{b},\widecheck{X}_{b}\right),\widecheck{u}_{b},\widecheck{X}_{b}\right).

(4.95)

For clarity, we denote
\mathfrak{p}\left(\widecheck{u}_{a},\widecheck{X}_{a}\right)
by
\mathfrak{p}_{a}
and
\mathfrak{p}\left(\widecheck{u}_{b},\widecheck{X}_{b}\right)
by
\mathfrak{p}_{b}
. We wish to estimate the
L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}
norm of the RHS of (
4.94
) and (
4.95
). We have

\displaystyle\left\|A\left(\mathfrak{p}_{a},\widecheck{u}_{a},\widecheck{X}_{a}\right)-A\left(\mathfrak{p}_{b},\widecheck{u}_{b},\widecheck{X}_{b}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}}

(4.96)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|{\mathcal{H}}\left(\bar{g}(\mathfrak{p}_{a}),\bar{\pi}(\mathfrak{p}_{a})\right)-{\mathcal{H}}\left(\bar{g}(\mathfrak{p}_{b}),\bar{\pi}(\mathfrak{p}_{b})\right)\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}}

\displaystyle\quad+\left\|{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathcal{H}}\left(\mathfrak{p}_{a},\widecheck{u}_{a},\widecheck{X}_{a}\right)-{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathcal{H}}\left(\mathfrak{p}_{b},\widecheck{u}_{b},\widecheck{X}_{b}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}}.

We start with the first term in (
4.96
) and use (
4.12
) to expand it:

\displaystyle\left\|{\mathcal{H}}\left(\bar{g}(\mathfrak{p}_{a}),\bar{\pi}(\mathfrak{p}_{a})\right)-{\mathcal{H}}\left(\bar{g}(\mathfrak{p}_{b}),\bar{\pi}(\mathfrak{p}_{b})\right)\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}}

(4.97)

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\left\|{\mathcal{H}}\left(e+\chi_{\vec{p}_{a}}(g_{\vec{p}_{a}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}_{a}}\pi_{\vec{p}_{a}}\right)-{\mathcal{H}}\left(e+\chi_{\vec{p}_{b}}(g_{\vec{p}_{b}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}_{b}}\pi_{\vec{p}_{b}}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\left\|D{\mathcal{H}}[e,0]\left(\breve{g}_{a}-\breve{g}_{b}\right)+\Delta\widecheck{g}_{ij}\left(\breve{g}_{a}^{ij}-\breve{g}_{b}^{ij}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}}+\left\|\mathrm{Err}_{1}(\mathfrak{p}_{a})-\mathrm{Err}_{1}(\mathfrak{p}_{b})\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}}

We estimate each term in (
4.97
).

•

For the first term, we use (
2.24
)

\displaystyle\left\|{\mathcal{H}}\left(e+\chi_{\vec{p}_{a}}(g_{\vec{p}_{a}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}_{a}}\pi_{\vec{p}_{a}}\right)-{\mathcal{H}}\left(e+\chi_{\vec{p}_{b}}(g_{\vec{p}_{b}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}_{b}}\pi_{\vec{p}_{b}}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\left(|m_{a}-m_{b}|+\eta^{2}|v_{a}-v_{b}|\right)\left\|r^{q+\delta-\frac{5}{2}}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\{\min(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})\leq r\leq 2\max(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})\}\right)}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\eta^{2}\left(|y_{a}-y_{b}|+|a_{a}-a_{b}|\right)\left\|r^{q+\delta-\frac{7}{2}}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\{\min(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})\leq r\leq 2\max(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})\}\right)}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\left(|m_{a}-m_{b}|+\eta^{2}|v_{a}-v_{b}|\right)\max(\lambda_{a},\lambda_{b})^{q+\delta-1}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\eta^{2}\left(|y_{a}-y_{b}|+|a_{a}-a_{b}|\right)\max(\lambda_{a},\lambda_{b})^{q+\delta-2}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim_{C_{0}}\eta^{1-2(q+\delta-1)\theta}\varepsilon^{2(q+\delta-1)\theta}\left(\left\|\widecheck{u}_{a}-\widecheck{u}_{b}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}+\left\|\widecheck{X}_{a}-\widecheck{X}_{b}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim_{C_{0}}\varepsilon\left(\left\|\widecheck{u}_{a}-\widecheck{u}_{b}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}+\left\|\widecheck{X}_{a}-\widecheck{X}_{b}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}\right),

where we also used (
4.71
), (
4.72
) and
2(q+\delta-1)\theta<1
.

•

For the second term, the support property of
\breve{g}_{a}-\breve{g}_{b}
simply implies

\displaystyle\left\|D{\mathcal{H}}[e,0]\left(\breve{g}_{a}-\breve{g}_{b}\right)+\Delta\widecheck{g}_{ij}\left(\breve{g}_{a}^{ij}-\breve{g}_{b}^{ij}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\left\|\breve{g}_{a}-\breve{g}_{b}\right\|_{W^{2,\infty}}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim_{C_{0}}\varepsilon\left(\left\|\widecheck{u}_{a}-\widecheck{u}_{b}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}+\left\|\widecheck{X}_{a}-\widecheck{X}_{b}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}\right),

where we used (
4.73
).

•

For the third term we use (
4.16
) and (
4.15
)

\displaystyle\left\|\mathrm{Err}_{1}(\mathfrak{p}_{a})-\mathrm{Err}_{1}(\mathfrak{p}_{b})\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\left\|\widecheck{g}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}+\left\|\widecheck{\pi}\right\|_{H^{1}_{-q-\delta}}\right)d(\mathfrak{p}_{a},\mathfrak{p}_{b})+\eta d(\mathfrak{p}_{a},\mathfrak{p}_{b})

\displaystyle\lesssim_{C_{0}}\varepsilon\left(\left\|\widecheck{u}_{a}-\widecheck{u}_{b}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}+\left\|\widecheck{X}_{a}-\widecheck{X}_{b}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}\right),

where we also used

\displaystyle d(\mathfrak{p}_{a},\mathfrak{p}_{b})

\displaystyle\lesssim_{C_{0}}\varepsilon\left(\left\|\widecheck{u}_{a}-\widecheck{u}_{b}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}+\left\|\widecheck{X}_{a}-\widecheck{X}_{b}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}\right),

(4.98)

which follows from (
4.71
), (
4.72
) and (
4.73
).

Collecting the above estimates, we have proved that

\displaystyle\left\|{\mathcal{H}}\left(\bar{g}(\mathfrak{p}_{a}),\bar{\pi}(\mathfrak{p}_{a})\right)-{\mathcal{H}}\left(\bar{g}(\mathfrak{p}_{b}),\bar{\pi}(\mathfrak{p}_{b})\right)\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}}\lesssim_{C_{0}}\varepsilon\left(\left\|\widecheck{u}_{a}-\widecheck{u}_{b}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}+\left\|\widecheck{X}_{a}-\widecheck{X}_{b}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}\right).

For the second term in (
4.96
) we use (
4.29
) and
\left|V_{\mathcal{O}}^{abcd}\right|\lesssim 1
to obtain

\displaystyle\left\|{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathcal{H}}\left(\mathfrak{p}_{a},\widecheck{u}_{a},\widecheck{X}_{a}\right)-{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathcal{H}}\left(\mathfrak{p}_{b},\widecheck{u}_{b},\widecheck{X}_{b}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{C_{0}}\left(\left\|\widecheck{u}_{a}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}+\left\|\widecheck{X}_{a}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}+\left\|\widecheck{u}_{b}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}+\left\|\widecheck{X}_{b}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}\right)d(\mathfrak{p}_{a},\mathfrak{p}_{b})

\displaystyle\quad+\eta^{2}\left(\left\|\widecheck{u}_{a}-\widecheck{u}_{b}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}+\left\|\widecheck{X}_{a}-\widecheck{X}_{b}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\left\|\widecheck{g}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}+\left\|\widecheck{\pi}\right\|_{H^{1}_{-q-\delta-1}}+\left\|\widecheck{u}_{a}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}+\left\|\widecheck{X}_{a}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}+\left\|\widecheck{u}_{b}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}+\left\|\widecheck{X}_{b}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt\times\left(\left\|\widecheck{u}_{a}-\widecheck{u}_{b}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}+\left\|\widecheck{X}_{a}-\widecheck{X}_{b}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\eta\varepsilon\left(\left\|\widecheck{u}_{a}-\widecheck{u}_{b}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}+\left\|\widecheck{X}_{a}-\widecheck{X}_{b}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}\right)+\eta\varepsilon d(\mathfrak{p}_{a},\mathfrak{p}_{b})

\displaystyle\lesssim_{C_{0}}\eta\varepsilon d(\mathfrak{p}_{a},\mathfrak{p}_{b})+\varepsilon\left(\left\|\widecheck{u}_{a}-\widecheck{u}_{b}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}+\left\|\widecheck{X}_{a}-\widecheck{X}_{b}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{C_{0}}\varepsilon\left(\left\|\widecheck{u}_{a}-\widecheck{u}_{b}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}+\left\|\widecheck{X}_{a}-\widecheck{X}_{b}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}\right),

where we used the continuous embeddings
H^{1}_{-q-\delta-1}\times H^{1}_{-q-\delta-1}\subset L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}
and
H^{2}_{-q-\delta}\times L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}\subset L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}
from Lemma
2.3
and (
4.98
). We have proved that

\displaystyle\left\|A\left(\mathfrak{p}_{a},\widecheck{u}_{a},\widecheck{X}_{a}\right)-A\left(\mathfrak{p}_{b},\widecheck{u}_{b},\widecheck{X}_{b}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{C_{0}}\varepsilon\left(\left\|\widecheck{u}_{a}-\widecheck{u}_{b}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}+\left\|\widecheck{X}_{a}-\widecheck{X}_{b}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}\right).

Repeating the argument, we can prove that

\displaystyle\left\|B\left(\mathfrak{p}_{a},\widecheck{u}_{a},\widecheck{X}_{a}\right)-B\left(\mathfrak{p}_{b},\widecheck{u}_{b},\widecheck{X}_{b}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{C_{0}}\varepsilon\left(\left\|\widecheck{u}_{a}-\widecheck{u}_{b}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}+\left\|\widecheck{X}_{a}-\widecheck{X}_{b}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}\right).

Applying Proposition
2.7
to the system (
4.94
)-(
4.95
) and using the orthogonality conditions of Corollary
4.16
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\Psi^{(s)}\left(\widecheck{u}_{a},\widecheck{X}_{a}\right)-\Psi^{(s)}\left(\widecheck{u}_{b},\widecheck{X}_{b}\right)\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{C_{0}}\varepsilon\left(\left\|\widecheck{u}_{a}-\widecheck{u}_{b}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}+\left\|\widecheck{X}_{a}-\widecheck{X}_{b}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}\right).

Taking
\varepsilon
small compared to
C_{0}^{-1}
shows that the solution map
\Psi^{(s)}
is a contraction for the distance in
H^{2}_{-q-\delta}
.
∎

The Banach fixed point theorem then implies the following statement on the system (
4.4
).

Corollary 4.22
.

If
\varepsilon
is small enough, there exists a unique solution
\widecheck{u}
and
\widecheck{X}
to the system (
4.4
) with the bound

\displaystyle\left\|\widecheck{u}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}+\left\|\widecheck{X}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}\lesssim\eta\varepsilon.

This concludes the proof of Theorem
3.8
, since the initial data set

\displaystyle\left(\left(1+\widecheck{u}\right)^{4}\bar{g}\left(\mathfrak{p}\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)\right),\bar{\pi}\left(\mathfrak{p}\left(\widecheck{u},\widecheck{X}\right)\right)+L_{e}\widecheck{X}\right)

solves the constraint equations on
{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
, and all the bounds stated in Theorem
3.8
follow from Corollaries
4.16
and
4.22
.

Appendix A
Appendix to Section
2

A.1
Proof of Proposition
2.7

We will use the following properties:

(i)

For
j\geq 1
, the functions
(w_{j,\ell})_{-(j-1)\leq\ell\leq j-1}
form a basis of
\mathfrak{H}^{j-1}
the set of harmonic polynomials defined on
{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
which are homogeneous of degree
j-1
.

(ii)

For
x=|x|\omega
and
y=|y|\omega^{\prime}
with
\omega,\omega^{\prime}\in\mathbb{S}^{2}
and
|x|>|y|
we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|x-y|}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2j+1}\frac{|y|^{j}}{|x|^{j+1}}\sum_{\ell=-j}^{j}(-1)^{\ell}Y_{j,-\ell}(\omega)Y_{j,\ell}(\omega^{\prime}).

(A.1)

We start with the first part of Proposition
2.7
, i.e. look at the scalar Laplace equation
\Delta u=f
. Note that the uniqueness of
\tilde{u}
follows from the injectivity of
\Delta:H^{2}_{-q-\delta}\longrightarrow L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}
(see Theorem
2.5
). It remains to show the existence of
\tilde{u}
. For each
j\geq 1
, we consider
G_{j}:{\mathbb{R}}_{+}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{R}}
a smooth compactly supported (in
[0,1]
) function and vanishing in a neighborhood of zero and such that
\int_{{\mathbb{R}}_{+}}G_{j}(r)r^{j+1}\mathrm{d}r=\frac{1}{4\pi}
. By using the fact that
(w_{j^{\prime},\ell^{\prime}})_{1\leq j^{\prime}\leq q,-(j^{\prime}-1)\leq\ell^{\prime}\leq j^{\prime}-1}
is a basis of
\bigcup_{i=0}^{q-1}\mathfrak{H}^{i}
, one can check that

\displaystyle f-\sum_{j=1}^{q}\sum_{\ell=-(j-1)}^{j-1}\langle f,w_{j,\ell}\rangle G_{j}(r)Y_{j-1,\ell}\in\left(\bigcup_{i=0}^{q-1}\mathfrak{H}^{i}\right)^{\perp_{L^{2}}}.

Moreover we have

\displaystyle\left\|f-\sum_{j=1}^{q}\sum_{\ell=-(j-1)}^{j-1}\langle f,w_{j,\ell}\rangle G_{j}(r)Y_{j-1,\ell}\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|f\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}}+\sum_{j=1}^{q}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|f|(1+r)^{j-1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|f\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}},

where we used

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|f|(1+r)^{j-1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|f|(1+r)^{q+\delta+\frac{1}{2}}(1+r)^{j-q-\delta-\frac{3}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|(1+r)^{j-q-\delta-\frac{3}{2}}\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|f\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|f\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}},

if
j\leq q
and
\delta>0
. Therefore we can apply Theorem
2.5
with
p=q
and
\rho=-q-\delta
and obtain the existence of
v
such that

\displaystyle\Delta v=f-\sum_{j=1}^{q}\sum_{\ell=-(j-1)}^{j-1}\langle f,w_{j,\ell}\rangle G_{j}(r)Y_{j-1,\ell},

with
\left\|v\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}\lesssim\left\|f\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}}
. Now, for
j\geq 1
and
-(j-1)\leq\ell\leq j-1
, we define

\displaystyle u_{j,\ell}=\frac{1}{4\pi}\left(G_{j}Y_{j-1,\ell}\right)*\frac{1}{|\cdot|},

which is such that
\Delta u_{j,\ell}=-G_{j}Y_{j-1,\ell}
. Let
x=r\omega
with
r>1
and
\omega\in\mathbb{S}^{2}
, since
G_{j}
is supported in
[0,1]
, we can use (
2.1
) and (
A.1
) to obtain

\displaystyle u_{j,\ell}(x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j^{\prime}=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{4\pi(2j^{\prime}+1)r^{j^{\prime}+1}}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\times\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=-j^{\prime}}^{j^{\prime}}\left(\int_{0}^{1}G_{j}(r^{\prime})(r^{\prime})^{j^{\prime}+2}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}\right)\left(\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}Y_{j-1,\ell}\left(\omega^{\prime}\right)Y_{j^{\prime},\ell^{\prime}}(\omega^{\prime}) \right)(-1)^{\ell^{\prime}}Y_{j^{\prime},-\ell^{\prime}}(\omega)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(2j-1)r^{j}}\left(\int_{0}^{1}G_{j}(r^{\prime})(r^{\prime})^{j+1}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}\right)(-1)^{\ell}Y_{j-1,-\ell}(\omega)

\displaystyle=-v_{j,\ell},

where
v_{j,\ell}
is introduced in Definition
2.4
. In particular, if we define
\tilde{u}_{j,\ell}=u_{j,\ell}+\chi(r)v_{j,\ell}
, then
\tilde{u}_{j,\ell}
is actually compactly supported and we just need to show that it is twice differentiable with bounded second order derivatives. This simply follows from its definition and the facts that
\frac{1}{|\cdot|}
is locally integrable and
G_{j}
supported outside of 0. In conclusion, the function
u
defined by

\displaystyle u=-\sum_{j=1}^{q}\sum_{\ell=-(j-1)}^{j-1}\langle f,w_{j,\ell}\rangle u_{j,\ell}+v

solves
\Delta u=f
and rewrites as

\displaystyle u=\chi(r)\sum_{j=1}^{q}\sum_{\ell=-(j-1)}^{j-1}\langle f,w_{j,\ell}\rangle v_{j,\ell}+\tilde{u},

where we defined

\displaystyle\tilde{u}=-\sum_{j=1}^{q}\sum_{\ell=-(j-1)}^{j-1}\langle f,w_{j,\ell}\rangle\tilde{u}_{j,\ell}+v.

Also we have from
\left\|v\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}\lesssim\left\|f\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}}
and
|\langle f,w_{j,\ell}\rangle|\lesssim\left\|f\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}}
that
\left\|\tilde{u}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}\lesssim\left\|f\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}}
. This concludes the proof of the first part of Proposition
2.7
.

We now look at the second part of Proposition
2.7
, i.e. the vector Laplace equation
\Delta X=Y
. Applying the first part of Proposition
2.7
to the three equations
\Delta X_{i}=Y_{i}
, we obtain the existence of a unique vector field
\tilde{X}
satisfying
\left\|\tilde{X}\right\|_{H^{2}_{-q-\delta}}\lesssim\left\|Y\right\|_{L^{2}_{-q-\delta-2}}
and such that

\displaystyle X=\chi(r)\sum_{j=1}^{q}\sum_{\ell=-(j-1)}^{j-1}\sum_{k=1,2,3}\langle Y,W_{j,\ell,k}\rangle V_{j,\ell,k}+\tilde{X}

solves
\Delta X=Y
. To conclude the proof, it remains to rearrange the terms corresponding to
j=2
. First, we recall the value of the
w_{2,\ell}
functions

\displaystyle w_{2,-1}=\sqrt{3}x^{2},\quad w_{2,0}=\sqrt{3}x^{3},\quad w_{2,1}=\sqrt{3}x^{1}.

Expressing each
x^{i}\partial_{j}
in the basis

\displaystyle\left(\Omega^{+}_{11},\Omega^{+}_{22},\Omega^{+}_{33},\Omega^{+}_{12},\Omega^{+}_{13},\Omega^{+}_{23},\Omega^{-}_{12},\Omega^{-}_{13},\Omega^{-}_{23}\right)

we obtain

\displaystyle W_{2,-1,1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}\left(\Omega^{+}_{12}-\Omega^{-}_{12}\right),

\displaystyle W_{2,0,1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}\left(\Omega^{+}_{13}-\Omega^{-}_{13}\right),

\displaystyle W_{2,1,1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}\Omega^{+}_{11},

\displaystyle W_{2,-1,2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}\Omega^{+}_{22},

\displaystyle W_{2,0,2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}\left(\Omega^{+}_{23}-\Omega^{-}_{23}\right),

\displaystyle W_{2,1,2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}\left(\Omega^{+}_{12}+\Omega^{-}_{12}\right),

\displaystyle W_{2,-1,3}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}\left(\Omega^{+}_{23}+\Omega^{-}_{23}\right),

\displaystyle W_{2,0,3}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}\Omega^{+}_{33},

\displaystyle W_{2,1,3}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}\left(\Omega^{+}_{13}+\Omega^{-}_{13}\right).

This gives

\displaystyle\frac{2}{\sqrt{3}}

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell=-1,0,1}\sum_{k=1,2,3}\langle Y,W_{2,\ell,k}\rangle V_{2,\ell,k}

\displaystyle=\langle Y,\Omega^{-}_{12}\rangle\left(-V_{2,-1,1}+V_{2,1,2}\right)+\langle Y,\Omega^{-}_{13}\rangle\left(-V_{2,0,1}+V_{2,1,3}\right)+\langle Y,\Omega^{-}_{23}\rangle\left(-V_{2,0,2}+V_{2,-1,3}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\langle Y,\Omega^{+}_{12}\rangle\left(V_{2,-1,1}+V_{2,1,2}\right)+\langle Y,\Omega^{+}_{13}\rangle\left(V_{2,0,1}+V_{2,1,3}\right)+\langle Y,\Omega^{+}_{23}\rangle\left(V_{2,0,2}+V_{2,-1,3}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\langle Y,\Omega^{+}_{11}\rangle V_{2,1,1}+\langle Y,\Omega^{+}_{22}\rangle V_{2,-1,2}+\langle Y,\Omega^{+}_{33}\rangle V_{2,0,3},

which concludes the proof of Proposition
2.7
.

A.2
Proof of Proposition
2.13

Identification of the kernels.

We recall the main result of
[
Mon75
]
: if
(M,{\bf g})
is a solution to the Einstein vacuum equations and if
(g,\pi)
are the induced data on a hypersurface
\Sigma
, then the kernel of
D\Phi[g,k]^{*}
(with
\Phi=({\mathcal{H}},{\mathcal{M}})
being the full constraint operator) consists of all Killing fields of
(M,{\bf g})
. More precisely, if
X
is Killing field of
(M,{\bf g})
, then
(h,Y)\in\ker\left(D\Phi[g,k]^{*}\right)
where the scalar
h
and the vector field
Y
are respectively the normal and tangential projection of
X
to
\Sigma
.

In the case of Minkowski spacetime, the 10-dimensional space of Killing fields is generated by the four translations, the three spatial rotations and the three Lorentz boosts. This proves that the kernels of
D{\mathcal{H}}[e,0]^{*}
and
D{\mathcal{M}}[e,0]^{*}
are given respectively by

\displaystyle\ker\left(D{\mathcal{H}}[e,0]^{*}\right)=\mathrm{Span}\left(w_{j,\ell},\;j=1,2,-(j-1)\leq\ell\leq j-1\right)

and

\displaystyle\ker\left(D{\mathcal{M}}[e,0]^{*}\right)=\mathrm{Span}\left(W_{1,0,1},W_{1,0,2},W_{1,0,3},\Omega^{-}_{12},\Omega^{-}_{13},\Omega^{-}_{23}\right)

as stated.

Construction of the family
(h_{j,\ell})_{3\leq j\leq q,-(j-1)\leq\ell\leq j-1}
.

Let
\mu=\mu(r)
be a smooth non-negative function compactly supported in
\{1\leq r\leq 10\}
. For
3\leq j\leq q
and
-(j-1)\leq\ell\leq j-1
we define

\displaystyle h_{j,\ell}

\displaystyle=\mu(r)D{\mathcal{H}}[e,0]^{*}(w_{j,\ell}).

By construction,
h_{j,\ell}
is a traceless (since
\Delta w_{j,\ell}=0
) smooth and compactly supported symmetric 2-tensor. Assume by contradiction that the matrix

\displaystyle\left(\left\langle h_{j,\ell},D{\mathcal{H}}[e,0]^{*}(w_{j^{\prime},\ell^{\prime}})\right\rangle\right)_{3\leq j,j^{\prime}\leq q,-(j-1)\leq\ell\leq j-1,-(j^{\prime}-1)\leq\ell^{\prime}\leq j^{\prime}-1}

(A.2)

is non-invertible. This implies that the columns of this matrix are linearly dependent, i.e. that there exists scalars
(\lambda_{j,\ell})_{3\leq j\leq q,-(j-1)\leq\ell\leq j-1}
such that

\displaystyle\sum_{j^{\prime}=3}^{q}\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=-(j^{\prime}-1)}^{j^{\prime}-1}\lambda_{j^{\prime},\ell^{\prime}}\left\langle\mu(r)D{\mathcal{H}}[e,0]^{*}(w_{j,\ell}),D{\mathcal{H}}[e,0]^{*}(w_{j^{\prime},\ell^{\prime}})\right\rangle=0

for all
(j,\ell)
such that
3\leq j\leq q
and
-(j-1)\leq\ell\leq j-1
and such that they are not all vanishing. We multiply each of these equalities by
\lambda_{j,\ell}
and sum over
(j,\ell)
to obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\mu(r)\sum_{j=3}^{q}\sum_{\ell=-(j-1)}^{j-1}\lambda_{j,\ell}D{\mathcal{H}}[e,0]^{*}(w_{j,\ell})\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=0.

Therefore, the sum
\sum_{j=3}^{q}\sum_{\ell=-(j-1)}^{j-1}\lambda_{j,\ell}D{\mathcal{H}}[e,0]^{*}(w_{j,\ell})
vanishes on
\{\mu\neq 0\}
and since each
D{\mathcal{H}}[e,0]^{*}(w_{j,\ell})
is a polynomial, this implies that

\displaystyle D{\mathcal{H}}[e,0]^{*}\left(\sum_{j=3}^{q}\sum_{\ell=-(j-1)}^{j-1}\lambda_{j,\ell}w_{j,\ell}\right)

\displaystyle=0.

Since the kernel of
D{\mathcal{H}}[e,0]^{*}
is generated by
\{w_{j,\ell},\;j=1,2,-(j-1)\leq\ell\leq j-1\}
, the linear independence of the family
\{w_{j,\ell},\;1\leq j\leq q,-(j-1)\leq\ell\leq j-1\}
implies that
\lambda_{j,\ell}=0
for all
3\leq j\leq q
and
-(j-1)\leq\ell\leq j-1
, which contradicts our first assumption. This shows that the matrix (
A.2
) is invertible. Moreover, since
\Delta w_{j,\ell}=0
and
\nabla\mu=\mu^{\prime}\partial_{r}
we have

\displaystyle(\mathrm{div}h_{j,\ell})_{r}

\displaystyle=\mu^{\prime}(r)(\mathrm{Hess}w_{j,\ell})_{rr}

\displaystyle=(j-1)(j-2)\mu^{\prime}(r)r^{j-3}Y_{j-1,\ell}.

The linear independence of the spherical harmonics thus implies the linear independence of the functions
(\mathrm{div}h_{j,\ell})_{r}
and thus of the 1-forms
\mathrm{div}h_{j,\ell}
.

Construction of the family
(\varpi_{Z})_{Z\in\mathcal{Z}_{q}}
.

We simplify the notations and rename the elements of
\mathcal{Z}_{q}
so that
\mathcal{Z}_{q}=\left\{Z_{1},\dots,Z_{N}\right\}
with
N=3q^{2}-6
. We define some cutoffs functions:

•

\mu^{(\infty)}
is simply defined by

\displaystyle\mu^{(\infty)}(r)=\mathbbm{1}_{[1,2]}(r).

•

for
n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
and
1\leq k\leq N
we define
\mu^{(n)}_{k}
as a smooth function satisfying
0\leq\mu^{(n)}_{k}\leq 1
and

\mu^{(n)}_{k}(r)=\left\{\begin{aligned} &1\qquad\text{if}\quad r\in\left[1+\frac{1}{kn},2-\frac{1}{kn}\right],\\
&0\qquad\text{if}\quad r\notin\left[1+\frac{1}{(k+1)n},2-\frac{1}{(k+1)n}\right].\end{aligned}\right.

We also define the following matrices:

\displaystyle M^{(\infty)}

\displaystyle=\left(\left\langle\mu^{(\infty)}D{\mathcal{M}}[e,0]^{*}(Z_{i}),D{\mathcal{M}}[e,0]^{*}(Z_{j})\right\rangle\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq N},

\displaystyle M^{(n)}

\displaystyle=\left(\left\langle\mu^{(n)}_{i}D{\mathcal{M}}[e,0]^{*}(Z_{i}),D{\mathcal{M}}[e,0]^{*}(Z_{j})\right\rangle\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq N}.

On the one hand, we can prove that
M^{(\infty)}
is invertible as we did above for the matrix (
A.2
) (the non-smoothness of
\mu^{(\infty)}
doesn’t affect the argument), ultimately using the description of the kernel of
D{\mathcal{M}}[e,0]^{*}
and the linear independence of the family
\left\{W_{1,0,1},W_{1,0,2},W_{1,0,3},\Omega^{-}_{12},\Omega^{-}_{13},\Omega^{-}_{23}\right\}\cup\mathcal{Z}_{q}
. On the other hand, by construction, for all
1\leq i\leq N
we have

\displaystyle\mu^{(n)}_{i}\xrightarrow[n\to+\infty]{\hskip 4.09723ptL^{2}\hskip 4.09723pt}\mu^{(\infty)}.

This implies the convergence

\displaystyle M^{(n)}\xrightarrow[n\to+\infty]{}M^{(\infty)}.

The set of invertible matrices being an open set, this proves that
M^{(n)}
is invertible for
n
large enough. Thus we take
n
large enough and define

\displaystyle\varpi_{i}

\displaystyle=\mu^{(n)}_{i}D{\mathcal{M}}[e,0]^{*}(Z_{i}).

Let us prove that the family
((\Delta\mathrm{tr}+\mathrm{div}\mathrm{div})\varpi_{i})_{1\leq i\leq N}
is linearly independent. Consider scalar
\lambda_{i}
such that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\lambda_{i}(\Delta\mathrm{tr}+\mathrm{div}\mathrm{div})\varpi_{i}=0.

(A.3)

Since the components in Cartesian coordinates of the vector fields
Z_{i}
are harmonic functions, one can show

\displaystyle(\Delta\mathrm{tr}+\mathrm{div}\mathrm{div})\varpi_{i}

\displaystyle=-(\mu^{(n)}_{i})^{\prime}\left(3\partial_{r}\mathrm{div}Z_{i}+\frac{3}{r}\mathrm{div}Z_{i}-\frac{1}{r}\partial_{r}(Z_{i})_{r}\right)-(\mu^{(n)}_{i})^{\prime\prime}\left(\mathrm{div}Z_{i}+\partial_{r}(Z_{i})_{r}\right).

In particular, each
(\Delta\mathrm{tr}+\mathrm{div}\mathrm{div})\varpi_{i}
is supported in
\left\{(\mu^{(n)}_{i})^{\prime}\neq 0\right\}
, i.e. in

\displaystyle S_{i}\vcentcolon=\left[1+\frac{1}{(i+1)n},1+\frac{1}{in}\right]\cup\left[2-\frac{1}{in},2-\frac{1}{(i+1)n}\right].

By construction, we have
\mathring{S}_{i}\cap\mathring{S}_{j}=\emptyset
if
i\neq j
. Therefore, (
A.3
) implies that

\displaystyle\lambda_{i}\left(-(\mu^{(n)}_{i})^{\prime}\left(3\partial_{r}\mathrm{div}Z_{i}+\frac{3}{r}\mathrm{div}Z_{i}-\frac{1}{r}\partial_{r}(Z_{i})_{r}\right)-(\mu^{(n)}_{i})^{\prime\prime}\left(\mathrm{div}Z_{i}+\partial_{r}(Z_{i})_{r}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=0

(A.4)

identically on
S_{i}
for all
1\leq i\leq N
. Assume that there exists
i
such that
\lambda_{i}\neq 0
, then (
A.4
) rewrites

\displaystyle Q_{i}(x)(\mu^{(n)}_{i})^{\prime\prime}(r)+P_{i}(x)(\mu^{(n)}_{i})^{\prime}(r)

\displaystyle=0

on
S_{i}
with
P_{i}
and
Q_{i}
polynomials. Since
Q_{i}
is a polynomial, there exists
\omega_{0}\in\mathbb{S}^{2}
such that
Q(r\omega_{0})\neq 0
for all
r>0
. Defining
f(r)=\frac{P_{i}(r\omega_{0})}{Q_{i}(r\omega_{0})}
we obtain

\displaystyle(\mu^{(n)}_{i})^{\prime\prime}(r)+f(r)(\mu^{(n)}_{i})^{\prime}(r)

\displaystyle=0

on
S_{i}
with
(\mu^{(n)}_{i})^{\prime}=0
on
\partial S_{i}
and
f
smooth. By Cauchy-Lipschitz, this would imply
(\mu^{(n)}_{i})^{\prime}=0
identically on
S_{i}
which is a contradiction. Therefore we necessarily have

\displaystyle 3\partial_{r}\mathrm{div}Z_{i}+\frac{3}{r}\mathrm{div}Z_{i}-\frac{1}{r}\partial_{r}(Z_{i})_{r}

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\mathrm{div}Z_{i}+\partial_{r}(Z_{i})_{r}

\displaystyle=0,

on
S_{i}
. Thus
\mathrm{div}Z_{i}
solves the equation

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\left(r^{\frac{4}{3}}\mathrm{div}Z_{i}\right)

\displaystyle=0.

Since
\mathrm{div}Z_{i}
is a polynomial, this is only possible if
\mathrm{div}Z_{i}=0
everywhere. From the second relation we then get
\partial_{r}(Z_{i})_{r}=0
which is a contradiction since

\displaystyle\partial_{r}(\Omega^{+}_{ab})_{r}

\displaystyle=2\frac{x^{a}x^{b}}{r^{2}},

\displaystyle\partial_{r}(W_{j,\ell,k})_{r}

\displaystyle=(j-1)r^{j-3}Y_{j-1,\ell}x^{k}.

Therefore,
\lambda_{i}=0
for all
1\leq i\leq N
and the family
\left((\Delta\mathrm{tr}+\mathrm{div}\mathrm{div})\varpi_{i}\right)_{1\leq i\leq N}
is linearly independent. This concludes the proof of Proposition
2.13
.

A.3
Proof of Proposition
2.19

Estimates on
\chi_{\vec{p}}
.

The cutoff
\chi_{\vec{p}}
is defined in Definition
2.18
and it satisfies the following properties:

(i)

we have
\left|\chi_{\vec{p}}\right|\lesssim\mathbbm{1}_{\{\lambda\leq r\}}
and if
n\geq 1
we have

\displaystyle r^{n}\left|\nabla^{n}\chi_{\vec{p}}\right|\lesssim_{n}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\lambda\leq r\leq 2\lambda\}},

(A.5)

(ii)

for all
n\geq 0
we have

\displaystyle r^{n}\left|\nabla^{n}\left(\chi_{\vec{p}}-\chi_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}}\right)\right|\lesssim_{n}\frac{\left|\lambda-\lambda^{\prime}\right|}{r}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\min(\lambda,\lambda^{\prime})\leq r\leq 2\max(\lambda,\lambda^{\prime})\}}.

(A.6)

We start with the proof of (
A.5
). From Faà di Bruno’s formula and
|\nabla^{k}r|\lesssim r^{1-k}
we get

\displaystyle\left|\nabla^{n}\chi_{\vec{p}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}r^{k-n}\lambda^{-n}\left|\chi^{(k)}\left(\frac{r}{\lambda}\right)\right|.

Since derivatives of
\chi
are supported in
\{1\leq r\leq 2\}
we obtain

\displaystyle r^{n}\left|\nabla^{n}\chi_{\vec{p}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{n}\left(\frac{r}{\lambda}\right)^{n}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\lambda\leq r\leq 2\lambda\}}.

\displaystyle\lesssim_{n}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\lambda\leq r\leq 2\lambda\}}.

We now prove (
A.6
), assuming without loss of generality that
\lambda\leq\lambda^{\prime}
. Let
n=0
. We can already assume that
\lambda\leq r\leq 2\lambda^{\prime}
otherwise
\chi_{\vec{p}}-\chi_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}}=0
. We distinguish several cases.

1.

If
2\lambda\leq\lambda^{\prime}\iff\frac{\lambda^{\prime}}{2}\leq\lambda^{\prime}-\lambda
. Thus, from
r\leq 2\lambda^{\prime}
we obtain
\frac{|\lambda-\lambda^{\prime}|}{r}\geq\frac{\lambda^{\prime}}{2r}\geq\frac{1}{4}
. Since
\left|\chi_{\vec{p}}-\chi_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}}\right|\leq 2
, we obtain
\left|\chi_{\vec{p}}-\chi_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}}\right|\lesssim\frac{|\lambda-\lambda^{\prime}|}{r}
.

2.

If
\lambda^{\prime}\leq 2\lambda
. We have

\displaystyle\left|\chi_{\vec{p}}-\chi_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{r}{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}(\lambda^{\prime}-\lambda)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{|\lambda-\lambda^{\prime}|}{r},

where we used
\frac{r}{\lambda^{\prime}}\leq 2
and
\frac{r}{\lambda}\leq 4
.

Since
\chi_{\vec{p}}-\chi_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}}
is supported in
\{\min(\lambda,\lambda^{\prime})\leq r\leq 2\max(\lambda,\lambda^{\prime})\}
, this concludes the proof of (
A.6
) if
n=0
. For
n\geq 1
, we use again Faà di Bruno’s formula and
|\nabla^{k}r|\lesssim r^{1-k}
to obtain

\displaystyle r^{n}\left|\nabla^{n}\left(\chi_{\vec{p}}(r)-\chi_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}}(r)\right)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left(\frac{r}{\lambda^{\prime}}\right)^{k}\left|\chi^{(k)}\left(\frac{r}{\lambda}\right)-\chi^{(k)}\left(\frac{r}{\lambda^{\prime}}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{k=1}^{n}r^{k}\left|\lambda^{-k}-(\lambda^{\prime})^{-k}\right|\left|\chi^{(k)}\left(\frac{r}{\lambda}\right)\right|.

For the first sum, we use the fact that
r\leq 2\lambda^{\prime}
and we apply the previous dichotomy argument to
\chi_{\vec{p}}^{(k)}(r)-\chi_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}}^{(k)}(r)
instead of
\chi_{\vec{p}}(r)-\chi_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}}(r)
. We obtain

\displaystyle\left|\chi_{\vec{p}}^{(k)}(r)-\chi_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}}^{(k)}(r)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{k}\frac{|\lambda-\lambda^{\prime}|}{r}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\min(\lambda,\lambda^{\prime})\leq r\leq 2\max(\lambda,\lambda^{\prime})\}}.

and thus

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left(\frac{r}{\lambda^{\prime}}\right)^{k}\left|\chi^{(k)}\left(\frac{r}{\lambda}\right)-\chi^{(k)}\left(\frac{r}{\lambda^{\prime}}\right)\right|\lesssim_{n}\frac{|\lambda-\lambda^{\prime}|}{r}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\min(\lambda,\lambda^{\prime})\leq r\leq 2\max(\lambda,\lambda^{\prime})\}}.

For the second sum, we use the fact that
\chi^{(k)}\left(\frac{r}{\lambda}\right)
with
k\geq 1
is supported in
\{\lambda\leq r\leq 2\lambda\}
to get

\displaystyle r^{k}\left|\lambda^{-k}-(\lambda^{\prime})^{-k}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{k}\frac{|\lambda-\lambda^{\prime}|}{r}\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\left(\frac{r}{\lambda}\right)^{j}\left(\frac{r}{\lambda^{\prime}}\right)^{k-j}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{k}\frac{|\lambda-\lambda^{\prime}|}{r}.

This concludes the proof of (
A.6
).

Estimates on
(g_{\vec{p}},\pi_{\vec{p}})
and
(e+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}})
.

For the proof of (
2.15
), we start by estimating
{\bf g}_{m,|a|}-{\bf m}
in the region
r\geq\lambda
. In this region, we have
\frac{|a|}{r}\leq\frac{1}{2}
and we deduce from the expression (see
[
Vis09
]
)

\displaystyle\tilde{r}=\sqrt{\frac{r^{2}-|a|^{2}+\sqrt{(r^{2}-|a|^{2})^{2}+4|a|^{2}z^{2}}}{2}}

that
1\lesssim\left|\nabla^{n}\left(\frac{\tilde{r}}{r}\right)\right|\lesssim 1
. After projecting the metric on
\{t=0\}
this implies

\displaystyle r^{n}\left|\nabla^{n}(g_{\vec{p}_{0}}-e)\right|+r^{n+1}\left|\nabla^{n}\pi_{\vec{p}_{0}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{n}\frac{m}{r},

(A.7)

where
\vec{p}_{0}=(m,0,|a|\partial_{3},0)
. Considering now the change of coordinates (
2.14
), we use

\displaystyle\gamma^{-1}\lesssim\left|\,{}^{(v)}\Lambda\right|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{4}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{4}}\lesssim\gamma

and that in the region
r\geq\lambda
we have

\displaystyle\left||x|^{-\ell}-|x-y|^{-\ell}\right|\lesssim|x|^{-\ell-1}.

This allows us to deduce from (
A.7
) that in the region
\{\lambda\leq r\}
we have

\displaystyle r^{n}\left|\nabla^{n}(g_{\vec{p}}-e)\right|+r^{n+1}\left|\nabla^{n}\pi_{\vec{p}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\gamma,n}\frac{m}{r}.

(A.8)

Using moreover the expression of
\,{}^{(v)}\Lambda
(see (8.2) in
[
ACR23
]
) and the fact that
x\mapsto\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-x^{2}}}
is locally Lipschitz we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\,{}^{(v)}\Lambda-\,^{(v^{\prime})}\Lambda\right|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{4}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{4}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|\gamma-\gamma^{\prime}\right|+\max(\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})\left|v-v^{\prime}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\max(\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})}\left|v-v^{\prime}\right|.

This implies that in the region
\{\max(\lambda,\lambda^{\prime})\leq r\}
we have the following difference estimate

\displaystyle r^{n}\left|\nabla^{n}\left(g_{\vec{p}}-g_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}}\right)\right|

\displaystyle+r^{n+1}\left|\nabla^{n}\left(\pi_{\vec{p}}-\pi_{\vec{p}\,^{\prime}}\right)\right|

(A.9)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\max(\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}),n}\frac{\left|m-m^{\prime}\right|}{r}+\max(m,m^{\prime})\left(\frac{\left|y-y^{\prime}\right|+\left|a-a^{\prime}\right|}{r^{2}}+\frac{\left|v-v^{\prime}\right|}{r}\right).

The estimate (
2.15
) then follows from putting (
A.5
) and (
A.8
) together, and the estimate (
2.16
) then follows from putting (
A.6
) and (
A.9
) together and using the bound

\displaystyle\left|\lambda-\lambda^{\prime}\right|\lesssim\left|y-y^{\prime}\right|+\left|a-a^{\prime}\right|.

The integral identities.

In order to prove (
2.19
)-(
2.22
) we introduce the classical asymptotic charges for an asymptotically flat initial data set:

\displaystyle{\bf E}(g,\pi)

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\frac{1}{16\pi}\lim_{R\to+\infty}\int_{S_{R}}\nu\cdot\left(\mathrm{div}g-\nabla\mathrm{tr}g\right),

\displaystyle{\bf P}(g,\pi)_{i}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\frac{1}{8\pi}\lim_{R\to+\infty}\int_{S_{R}}\pi_{\nu i},

\displaystyle{\bf J}(g,\pi)_{i}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\frac{1}{8\pi}\lim_{R\to+\infty}\int_{S_{R}}\pi_{Y^{i}\nu},

\displaystyle{\bf C}(g,\pi)_{i}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\lim_{R\to+\infty}\int_{S_{R}}x^{i}\nu\cdot\left(\mathrm{div}g-\nabla\mathrm{tr}g\right)-\lim_{R\to+\infty}\int_{S_{R}}(g_{\nu i}-\nu^{i}\mathrm{tr}g),

where
S_{r}
is the Cartesian sphere of radius
r
and
\nu
is the outward pointing unit normal to
S_{r}
. Following
[
CD03
]
and Section 8 of
[
ACR23
]
we find that the asymptotic charges of
(g_{\vec{p}},\pi_{\vec{p}})
are given in terms of
\vec{p}
by

\displaystyle\left({\bf E},{\bf P},{\bf J},{\bf C}\right)(g_{\vec{p}},\pi_{\vec{p}})=\left(\gamma m,-\gamma mv,ma,my\right).

(A.10)

We start with (
2.19
) and (
2.20
), i.e. the identities involving vector fields growing like 1 at infinity. Thanks to Lemma
2.10
and since
\Phi(e,0)=0
we have

\displaystyle\Phi\left(e+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}\right)

\displaystyle=D\Phi[e,0]\left(\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}\right)+{\mathcal{R}}_{\Phi,\mathrm{bh}}(\vec{p}),

(A.11)

where
\Phi
denotes
{\mathcal{H}}
or
{\mathcal{M}}
and where the error term
{\mathcal{R}}_{\Phi,\mathrm{bh}}(\vec{p})
is schematically given by

\displaystyle{\mathcal{R}}_{\Phi,\mathrm{bh}}(\vec{p})

\displaystyle=\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e)\nabla^{2}(\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e))+(\nabla(\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e)))^{2}+(\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}})^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e)\nabla(\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}})+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e)(\nabla(\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e)))^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+(\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e)\nabla(\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e)))^{2}+(\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e)\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}})^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e)\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}\nabla(\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e))+(\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e))^{2}\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}\nabla(\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e)).

Thanks to the assumption
m\leq 1
and (
2.15
) we have

\displaystyle\left|{\mathcal{R}}_{\Phi,\mathrm{bh}}(\vec{p})\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\gamma}\frac{m^{2}}{r^{4}}.

(A.12)

Using
m,m^{\prime}\leq 1
and (
2.16
) we also obtain

\displaystyle\left|{\mathcal{R}}_{\Phi,\mathrm{bh}}(\vec{p})-{\mathcal{R}}_{\Phi,\mathrm{bh}}(\vec{p}\,^{\prime})\right|

(A.13)

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim_{\max(\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})}\frac{\max(m,m^{\prime})}{r^{3}}\left(\frac{|m-m^{\prime}|}{r}+\max(m,m^{\prime})\left(\frac{|y-y^{\prime}|+|a-a^{\prime}|}{r^{2}}+\frac{|v-v^{\prime}|}{r}\right)\right).

From (
A.11
) and the fact that
\Phi\left(e+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}\right)
is supported in
\{\lambda\leq r\leq 2\lambda\}
(since
\Phi(e,0)=0
and
\Phi(g_{\vec{p}},\pi_{\vec{p}})=0
away from the center of mass
y
) we obtain

\displaystyle\left\langle{\mathcal{H}}\left(e+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}\right),1\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle D{\mathcal{H}}[e,0]\left(\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e)\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{\lambda\leq r\}},1\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\left\langle{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{H}},\mathrm{bh}}(\vec{p})\mathbbm{1}_{\{\lambda\leq r\}},1\right\rangle,

\displaystyle\left\langle{\mathcal{M}}\left(e+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}\right),W_{1,0,k}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle D{\mathcal{M}}[e,0]\left(\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{\lambda\leq r\}},W_{1,0,k}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\left\langle{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{M}},\mathrm{bh}}(\vec{p})\mathbbm{1}_{\{\lambda\leq r\}},W_{1,0,k}\right\rangle.

Thanks to (
2.10
) and Stokes theorem we have

\displaystyle\left\langle D{\mathcal{H}}[e,0]\left(\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e)\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{\lambda\leq r\}},1\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad=\lim_{R\to+\infty}\int_{\{\lambda\leq r\leq R\}}\left(-\Delta\mathrm{tr}\left(\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e)\right)+\mathrm{div}\mathrm{div}\left(\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e)\right)\right)

\displaystyle\quad=\lim_{R\to+\infty}\int_{\partial\{\lambda\leq r\leq R\}}\nu\cdot\left(-\nabla\mathrm{tr}\left(\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e)\right)+\mathrm{div}\left(\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e)\right)\right),

where
\nu
denotes the outward pointing unit normal to
\partial\{\lambda\leq r\leq R\}
. Now, note that
\partial\{\lambda\leq r\leq R\}=S_{\lambda}\cup S_{R}
and that
\chi_{\vec{p}}=0
on
S_{\lambda}
,
\chi_{\vec{p}}=1
on
S_{R}
(since
R\geq 2\lambda
) and
\nu\chi_{\vec{p}}=0
on
S_{\lambda}\cup S_{R}
. This implies

\displaystyle\left\langle D{\mathcal{H}}[e,0]\left(\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e)\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{\lambda\leq r\}},1\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\lim_{R\to+\infty}\int_{S_{R}}\nu\cdot\left(-\nabla\mathrm{tr}g_{\vec{p}}+\mathrm{div}g_{\vec{p}}\right).

We recognize the expression of
16\pi{\bf E}(g_{\vec{p}},\pi_{\vec{p}})
and obtain

\displaystyle\left\langle{\mathcal{H}}\left(e+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}\right),1\right\rangle

\displaystyle=16\pi\gamma m+\left\langle{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{H}},\mathrm{bh}}(\vec{p})\mathbbm{1}_{\{\lambda\leq r\}},1\right\rangle.

We define
{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{H}},1}(\vec{p})\vcentcolon=\left\langle{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{H}},\mathrm{bh}}(\vec{p})\mathbbm{1}_{\{\lambda\leq r\}},1\right\rangle
. The estimates (
A.12
)-(
A.13
) imply

\displaystyle\left|{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{H}},1}(\vec{p})\right|\lesssim_{\gamma}m^{2}

and

\displaystyle\left|{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{H}},1}(\vec{p})-{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{H}},1}(\vec{p}\,^{\prime})\right|

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\left|\left\langle\left({\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{H}},\mathrm{bh}}(\vec{p})-{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{H}},\mathrm{bh}}(\vec{p}\,^{\prime})\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{\lambda\leq r\}},1\right\rangle\right|+\left|\left\langle{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{H}},\mathrm{bh}}(\vec{p}\,^{\prime})\left(\mathbbm{1}_{\{\lambda\leq r\}}-\mathbbm{1}_{\{\lambda^{\prime}\leq r\}}\right),1\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim_{\max(\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})}\max(m,m^{\prime})\left(|m-m^{\prime}|+\max(m,m^{\prime})\left(|y-y^{\prime}|+|a-a^{\prime}|+|v-v^{\prime}|\right)\right)

\displaystyle\quad+(m^{\prime})^{2}|\lambda-\lambda^{\prime}|

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim_{\max(\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})}\max(m,m^{\prime})\left(|m-m^{\prime}|+\max(m,m^{\prime})\left(|y-y^{\prime}|+|a-a^{\prime}|+|v-v^{\prime}|\right)\right),

where we used the fact that
(1+r)^{-4}
is integrable on
{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
. Moreover, thanks to (
2.12
) and to the following version of Stokes theorem

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}X\cdot\mathrm{div}T

\displaystyle=\int_{\partial\Omega}T_{X\nu}-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}\nabla\otimes X\cdot T,

we have

\displaystyle\left\langle D{\mathcal{M}}[e,0]\left(\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{\lambda\leq r\}},W_{1,0,k}\right\rangle=\lim_{R\to+\infty}\int_{S_{R}}(\pi_{\vec{p}})_{k\nu},

where we recall from Proposition
2.13
that
\nabla\otimes W_{1,0,k}=0
. We recognize the expression of
8\pi{\bf P}(g_{\vec{p}},\pi_{\vec{p}})_{k}
and obtain

\displaystyle\left\langle{\mathcal{M}}\left(e+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}\right),W_{1,0,k}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-8\pi\gamma mv^{k}+\left\langle{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{M}},\mathrm{bh}}(\vec{p})\mathbbm{1}_{\{\lambda\leq r\}},W_{1,0,k}\right\rangle.

We define
{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{M}},W_{1,0,k}}(\vec{p})\vcentcolon=\left\langle{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{M}},\mathrm{bh}}(\vec{p})\mathbbm{1}_{\{\lambda\leq r\}},W_{1,0,k}\right\rangle
and (
A.12
)-(
A.13
) again imply

\displaystyle\left|{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{M}},W_{1,0,k}}(\vec{p})\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\gamma}m^{2}

and

\displaystyle\left|{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{M}},W_{1,0,k}}(\vec{p})-{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{M}},W_{1,0,k}}(\vec{p}\,^{\prime})\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\lesssim_{\max(\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})}\max(m,m^{\prime})\left(|m-m^{\prime}|+\max(m,m^{\prime})\left(|y-y^{\prime}|+|a-a^{\prime}|+|v-v^{\prime}|\right)\right).

This concludes the proof of (
2.19
) and (
2.20
).

We now turn to the proof of (
2.21
) and (
2.22
), i.e. the identities involving vector fields growing like
r
at infinity. Compared to the proof of (
2.19
) and (
2.20
), we need to isolate the quadratic terms in the expansion of the constraint operators:

\displaystyle\left\langle{\mathcal{H}}\left(e+\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}\right),x^{k}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle D{\mathcal{H}}[e,0]\left(\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e)\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{\lambda\leq r\}},x^{k}\right\rangle

(A.14)

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\left\langle\frac{1}{2}D^{2}{\mathcal{H}}[e,0]\left(\left(\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}\right),\left(\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}\right)\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{\lambda\leq r\}},x^{k}\right\rangle}_{=\vcentcolon Q^{k}(\vec{p})}

\displaystyle\quad+\left\langle\mathrm{Err}{\mathcal{H}}[e,0]\left(\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{\lambda\leq r\}},x^{k}\right\rangle.

As above we can show that

\displaystyle\left\langle D{\mathcal{H}}[e,0]\left(\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e)\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{\lambda\leq r\}},x^{k}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=my^{k},

and using Lemma
2.10
, (
2.15
) and (
2.16
) we can also show that

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\mathrm{Err}{\mathcal{H}}[e,0]\left(\chi_{\vec{p}}(g_{\vec{p}}-e),\chi_{\vec{p}}\pi_{\vec{p}}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{\lambda\leq r\}},x^{k}\right\rangle\right|\lesssim m^{3}

and
